### Settled

- **LNK-INTERREPLICA retired.** SPEC-1 (a)/(b) delete its §28.3 row and paragraph; `CH-HOLDERFORWARD` takes Link `None`, keeps provenance C19, and its card Endpoint carries the address [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1].
- **§28.3 link rule.** A link entry exists only for a connection more than one channel row refers to, or one no channel rides yet; a one-row connection reads `None`. LNK-GWCONTROL qualifies through CH-MCP-PLATFORM and CH-MCP-CONNECTOR.
- **Problem statement "widen the link".** Met in intent because no new link is declared; the reason sits in the summary's What changes bullet for SPEC-1; the problem statement is not edited.
- **Single-channel card format.** Copy the §28.5.5/§28.5.7 `None` lead wording; card bullet reads `- **Link.** \`None\` (§28.3).`
- **CH-HOLDERFORWARD authority direction.** `Forwarding replica` in both dial and authority columns; §28.2 defines the axis as the message originator (as CH-OBJSTORE, CH-LLMPROXY, CH-ADMISSION).
- **C19 provenance.** Valid: gateway-runtime-comms.md gateway-to-gateway entry; not a breach of the post-inventory `None` rule.
- **Card Endpoint has no default port.** "default 50054" dropped; the default lives only in the SPEC-7 (b) §13.2.4 Gateway row and CHART-1 values.
- **Port and NET id free.** 50054, `gateway.interReplicaPort`, and NET-072 appear nowhere in spec/, charts/, or docs/; 50051/50052/50053 are taken; no port inventory exists outside §13.2.4.
- **Gateway certificate.** Leaf carries server auth and client auth and DNS SAN `lenny-gateway.lenny-system.svc`, already used as a TLS client to the Token Service; symmetric NET-072 works.
- **Kubernetes surface.** No CRD, finalizer, webhook, or controller; only the §13.2.4 NetworkPolicy self-peer, pod-IP addressing, and the cert SAN.
- **§28.7 needs no row.** CH-HOLDERFORWARD is HTTP and adds no schemas/ artifact.
- **Plan scope and declines.** Scope is the plan §10.2 "Inter-replica carrier proposal" plus carrier parts of R10, R13, R18, R19, R21; gRPC/proto, REG-COORDMIRROR routing, latency budget, circuit breaker, and R21's concurrent-slot leg are declined in summary D1, D2, D14, and Non-goals; no plan finding in rounds 1 and 7.
- **No §7.2 inbox target.** The plan's "R19 rewrites the §7.2 inbox sentence" has no target; the off-holder fallback lived only in §29.3, which SPEC-5 (a)-(c) replaces.
- **Holder value format.** `<replica identity>.<process token>@<address>`, token random once per process inside `coordinationHolder`; stated only in SPEC-2 (b) **Replica address:**; design prose, SPEC-1 (e), and D2 point at it.
- **Mirror carries holder value.** REG-COORDMIRROR `coordinator_replica` carries it; SPEC-2 (c) swaps §10.1.8 step 1 `$this_replica_id` for `$this_holder_value`; CODE-1 item 9 (**Comments.**) swaps the copies in migrations/0164 and coordlease.go; CODE-1 Targets name coordlease.go.
- **Per-replica Sweeper.** The "Gateway sweeper" REG-COORDMIRROR writer runs in each replica and upserts rows for its own leases, so SPEC-2 (b)'s two writer statements agree (USEFUL three times).
- **§29.7/§29.8 "this replica".** Stays true under the holder value; 0100 territory; not an edit site.
- **Two Postgres lease tables.** Fallback leases are `session_leases` (migrations/0111); `coordination_lease` (0164) is the REG-COORDMIRROR mirror; SPEC-2 (a)'s lease row matches shipped code (D19), no new write load.
- **Crash takeover.** Sweeper-driven adoption on lease lapse (§10.1.1 Primary); an absent holder is transient, so rule 6 failing closed on it strands nothing.
- **Pod-IP reuse.** Covered by the rule 5 lease check and NET-072 SAN validation; a container restart keeps the IP and draws a new token.
- **CH-BINDRELEASE predicate home.** New SPEC-6 (c) sentence in §11.4 step 2: release only when the row on the Postgres primary is terminal; card, §28.6, §28.8 cite it.
- **Code-only release details.** Self-skip (Origin), publishing-replica field, user field, and the terminal-claim once-guard are not written into spec.
- **Release payload.** Code sends `{tenantId,userId,origin,sessionIds}`; SPEC-6 (b) "names the tenant and the sessions" is accurate.
- **`lenny:session:terminate` registration.** §12.4 platform-scoped row after `lenny:events:{session_id}`, plus the exception-category sentence, wrapper prefix list (with "and"), and isolation parenthesis; §12.6 drops "Three", lists the fan-out, and property (2) keeps polling fallback for circuit breaker and deny list only (SPEC-6 (d), (e)).
- **Channel name was code-only.** Before SPEC-6 it appeared only in propagator.go.
- **Full-revoke ordering.** §11.4 step 2 writes `cancelled` before release or publish, so the primary-terminal gate never blocks it and a forged or stale publish cannot release a live binding.
- **§12.8 drain.** Runs in the gateway process (Gateway writer correct); a drain publish lost to an erasure-job crash is not re-published (accepted at-most-once); the propagator skips its own publish, so the drain replica relies on local release (CODE-3 item 4).
- **CH-BINDRELEASE Degradation.** States only at-most-once delivery and "A dropped request leaves the binding on the replica that holds it."; §28.8 cell reads "The publish is not redelivered (§28.5.7)" [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1].
- **CH-BINDRELEASE Endpoint.** Names the deployment's Redis endpoint and cites the CH-EVENTRELAY **Endpoint.** bullet only for TLS, AUTH, and client-cert requirements; both rows keep Link `None` [spec.7.fix-design-G1.1].
- **Holder-side release absent.** The code Sweeper's ReleaseTerminalBinding is unspecified (D17, summary **Unspecified backstop release.**); §5.2 does state a gateway-leader stale slot-member release.
- **Reclaimers.** §4.6.1 orphan detection drains only a pod no active session references; §5.2 stale-member release covers a pod another session names, after claimOrphanTimeout; after §12.8 erasure deletes the row on a shared pod neither fires.
- **SPEC-9 deleted.** The §12.8 hold's home is 0100's §12.8 text; the delivered-request read is §11.4 step 2 plus SPEC-6 (c).
- **0100 window vs dropped publish.** 0100's post-terminal window ends at the release record or the terminal pipeline budget, so the §12.8 hold does not protect a dropped publish.
- **0100 leaves anchors intact.** 0100's landed spec (impl-0100, 52af0cd90) touches none of 0101's anchors; SPEC-6 (a) applies in either order.
- **Rule 2 what is forwarded.** The request the handler received; a tool with no §15.1 route (`lenny/send_message`, `lenny/cancel_session`) forwards as its own `tools/call`; the handler returns the coordinator's response (§15.2.1).
- **Rule 3 calling session.** One peer-asserted principal, `X-Lenny-Calling-Session`, read only on the forward listener SPEC-7 restricts to gateway pods; "again" removed from rule 3, D5, and the design overview.
- **Rule 4 skip list.** Coordinator skips §11.1 rate limits, idempotency, and §4.8 PreToolResult; PreToolResult runs on the serving replica alone.
- **Rule 6 DLQ key.** Presence of `inReplyTo`; any message carrying one fails closed 409; forward kind `kindMessageNoReplyTo`; rule 6 is the predicate's home, SPEC-5 (b) row and §28.8 cell point at it, D10 holds the reason.
- **Rule 6 pre-hold checks.** The replica applying rule 6 runs the authorization, scope, and rate-limit checks of the route or tool, refuses as they state, else enqueues; rule 5 inherits; wording matches §7.2 path 6 **Admission.**
- **Rule 7 dedup.** Limited to a sender-supplied message `id` (§15.4); an id-less message can be delivered twice; no dedup added.
- **SPEC-5 (e).** §11.5 is the home of "a rule 6/7 TARGET_NOT_READY is not stored"; rule 7 dropped its Idempotency-Key clause.
- **§11.5 reach.** Covers CreateSession, FinalizeWorkspace, StartSession, SpawnChild, Approve/DenyDelegation, Resume; message send is outside it; held-pod resume is the only forwarding row (e) reaches; middleware AllowedPaths are `/v1/sessions/{id}/resume` and `/mcp`.
- **Idempotency middleware behaviour.** Caches every non-5xx, non-streamed response 24h; `maybePersist` has no decline hook and `r` passes unchanged; skip reasons live only in Go doc comments; §11.5 states no 5xx/streamed exception.
- **SPEC-5 (d) interaction record.** Store-and-wake kept; row defines the record (Postgres, keyed tenant/session/user/call-or-elicitation id, written before surfaced), first-wins pending-conditional write, refusal cited to §7.2 and §9.2, wake by the replica whose call is blocked.
- **Interaction store in code.** pgstore.Resolve selects FOR UPDATE and returns ErrNotFound/ErrAlreadyResolved; Put precedes the SSE publish; tool-approval wake is a 25 ms Postgres poll.
- **Refusal codes.** §7.2 approve gives 404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND; §9.2/§15.1 give 404 ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND.
- **SPEC-5 (f).** §29.2 Session start step 9 boundary sentence appended as (f); heading gains "§ 29.2 Session start"; summary deliverable index names it.
- **Gateway-to-store count sites.** Only the §28.5.7 lead and §29.2 step 9; SPEC-4 (a) and SPEC-5 (f) cover both.
- **Identifier sweep clean.** After SPEC-1/3/4 (h)/5 (a)/8 no spec `LNK-INTERREPLICA` or `ForwardMessage` remains (sites in §28.3, §28.5.4, §28.8 closing, §29.3, §18.19); docs/, schemas/, charts/ carry none.
- **Anchors resolve.** Every staged anchor resolves uniquely in draft-0101 and impl-0100 (round 7); CH-OBJSTORE/CH-EVENTRELAY anchors match twice and each sub-edit names its table.
- **SPEC-4 lettering.** After deleting the old SPEC-4 (f), old (g)/(h)/(i) became (f)/(g)/(h); locate §28.8 rows by content.
- **§28.6/§28.8 restate cards by design.** A cell or sentence that restates a card or §29.3 rule with a citation is the convention, not a rule-(g) copy; "names no metric" is also convention.
- **Agent send path.** Agent `lenny/send_message` arrives as `CallPlatformTool` on LNK-GWCONTROL; Bridge builds the principal from the session row with no credential; no spec section states a platform call's caller identity.
- **send_message handler.** One registration serves edge `/mcp` and the platform path; order is row read, cross-tenant, messagingScope, msg id, rate limit, inReplyTo, PreMessageDelivery, `mcpSend.route`.
- **Edge /mcp stateless.** A forwarded `tools/call` needs no session handshake.
- **PreToolResult location.** Installed in mcptools (`buildPreToolResultInterceptor`), not cmd/lenny-gateway; `sessionserver.Forwarded(ctx)` adds no import cycle; both handleToolCall and DispatchTool apply it.
- **Platform tool table.** §9.1 defines `lenny/*` tools; §15.2 lists client tools only; citing §15.2 for a platform tool is off-target.
- **messagingRateLimit.** A §8.3 delegation-lease limit, not §11.1, so rule 4 does not exempt the coordinator from it.
- **PreMessageDelivery at delivery.** Fires on the coordinator at delivery, so a DLQ-held message still passes it; rule 6 need not list it (USEFUL).
- **currentState exposure.** Rule 6/7 409 returning `currentState` to an authenticated-only caller is pre-existing handler behaviour (USEFUL; closes the spec.4 OPEN).
- **Off-holder 409 detail.** Code already answers with `details.currentState`; SPEC-5 aligns spec to code.
- **TARGET_NOT_READY catalog.** §15.1 row covers only pre-running; refuted as polish earlier.
- **DLQ is Redis-only.** An outage gives `inbox_unavailable` per §7.2 path 6; no regression; `queued` already denotes a DLQ hold.
- **Held-message delivery points.** Idle suspension, leader Backlog resume, and takeover deliver DLQ entries (§7.2 **Delivery of held messages.**).
- **Suspended messages unforwarded.** §7.2 path 6 keeps them local; SPEC-5 keeps that.
- **Rule 8 deadline.** `gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds`, 30 s.
- **Dual-store and dev mode.** Dual-store does not conflict with rule 1; §17.4 item 3 covers the dev-mode no-forward clause.
- **msg_dedup unimplemented.** §15.4 dedup has no pkg/ code; rule 1 implies the coordinator records a forwarded id.
- **§12.4 failure table.** Has no pub/sub rows at all; pre-existing gap.
- **No client surface for holder value.** Absent from OpenAPI, docs/api, and sdks.
- **Performance and security rounds.** No findings in round 4 (performance) or round 2 (security); forwarding adds one Redis GET per off-holder request and one primary read per held binding per release.
- **0100 location.** The draft-0101 worktree predates 0100; read it from /home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/impl-0100/proposals/0100_* or /home/ec2-user/lenny/proposals/0100_* (USEFUL).

### Traps

- **Do not bring back LNK-INTERREPLICA.** No `LNK-INTERREPLICA` row and no "widen the link" wording: one referring channel row breaks §28.3 and tests/tier11_docs/spec_28_register_writers_test.go `minimumReferringChannelRows = 2`. Withdrawn: amending §28.3 to admit "a link declared before its first channel row" (exception clause, edits a gated rule, needs a second exemption class); justifying the link by a future channel (release rides Redis, interactions are store-and-wake).
- **Shared Redis connection wording.** Never write "the same connection" or "the Redis connection the CH-EVENTRELAY bullet states" for CH-BINDRELEASE: under §28.3 that puts two rows on one connection while both read `None`. Say "endpoint" or "requirements". Withdrawn: an LNK- entry for the gateway Redis connection (no shared connection exists; contradicts the rule that retired LNK-INTERREPLICA).
- **"Terminal claim".** In spec it is a SandboxClaim disposition term (§4.6.1, §6.2); the release once-guard (terminalClaimRecord, BeginTerminal) is code-only. Do not put it in the CH-BINDRELEASE card, §28.6, or §28.8, and do not define it in spec. The old SPEC-4 (f) §28.6 scoping sentence was deleted for this.
- **CH-BINDRELEASE reclaim statement.** Failed three times: a false universal ("no such path" / "reclaimed by orphan GC", round 3), a §4.6.1 + §5.2 pointer (round 4), and a qualifier. Do not add any reclaim statement, qualifier, enumeration, or pointer to the card or its §28.8 row. After §12.8 erasure deletes the row on a shared pod, §5.2 (needs a terminal row past claimOrphanTimeout) and §4.6.1 (needs no active session referencing the pod) both miss it. Three round-5 reviewers called the pointer accurate; they missed the erasure case.
- **MISTAKE: answering a reclaim finding with another reclaim claim.** Rounds 3 and 4 each did this and spent two rounds of findings on one sentence. The gap is already recorded in summary **Unspecified backstop release.** and problem statement "indefinitely while another active session references it".
- **SPEC-9 dropped-request wording.** Do not re-add a card sentence saying the post-terminal window protects a dropped publish: 0100's window closes at the budget with no release recorded and §12.8 erasure proceeds with the binding held. Withdrawn: rewriting SPEC-9 to "a delivered request finds the terminal row" (second home for §12.8/§11.4).
- **Pinned-restart holder value.** With LENNY_REPLICA_ID pinned, identity, pod IP, and port survive a container restart; without the token the Sweeper renews the predecessor's lease as its own (`priorHolder == s.replicaID`, no CAS or fence) and `coordinatesSession` answers true with no binding. Withdrawn: deleting the restart sentence (accepts an unfenced self-renew); token only when pinned (two modes); suffix in resolveReplicaID (breaks D3 nonce checkpoint); mirror on the bare id (pinned restart barriers predecessor sessions); copying the format into SPEC-1 (e)/D2.
- **§10.1.8 query copies.** The query is copied in coordlease.go package doc and migrations/0164 comments; update with the spec. `grep -c "\$this_replica_id"` in double quotes returns 0; use `grep -F`.
- **§29.3 MCP row vs rule 2.** The row "every other session-addressed tool call ... the REST route the tool maps onto" governs whether a call forwards; rule 2 governs what is forwarded. Do not edit the row to match rule 2.
- **cancel_session parity watch-out.** Deleted from the summary because the coordinator now runs the tool's own authorization; do not restore it.
- **send_message forward placement.** The forward goes at the handler entry, before scope, rate limit, inReplyTo, and PreMessageDelivery; on not-delivered the handler runs them locally and holds (CODE-2 item 6). Do not move it after those steps. Withdrawn: "render REST into MCP format"; REST route plus sender header; forwarding after local checks at `mcpSend.route` (coordinator trusts peer outcome, checks run twice); always DLQ-holding platform sends; failing closed (breaks two-replica messaging).
- **Rule 3 withdrawn options.** Relaying the adapter SA token (pool-wide, does not name the session) and failing closed on off-holder platform sends (half of sends fail on two replicas).
- **callerSessionID fallback.** Falls back to client-supplied `fromSessionId` when the principal has no SessionID, so an edge `/mcp` client can name any sender. Pre-existing, outside 0101, candidate BUILD-GAPS finding.
- **"Matching inReplyTo" is unevaluable on the serving replica.** The pending request_input wait is coordinator-process-only; never key a serving-side predicate on a match. TEST-2 item 3's clause "including for an `inReplyTo` that matches no outstanding call" separates presence from match; do not cut it. Withdrawn: renaming the SPEC-5 (b) row label (it is the find-anchor); shared pending-input storage (R20).
- **SPEC-5 (e) withdrawn homes.** Rule 7 as home (contradicts §11.5); answering 5xx/503 (changes the reused code); middleware body-parsing every TARGET_NOT_READY (catches the §15.1 pre-running case). Do not merge the idempotency `DoNotStore` gate with `sessionserver.Forwarded(ctx)`: the gate is on the serving replica, the mark on the coordinator.
- **docs/api/rest.md Idempotency.** It mirrors §11.5 **Mechanism.** and names only 5xx as uncached. Round 1 design judged it not a site; the later follow-up fix deferred a docs edit (see Deferred). Any §11.5 caching change needs a look there.
- **Interaction row wording.** "the replica whose call is blocked on the record" is deliberate; never "the coordinating replica's wait": a chained elicitation's wait runs on the raising session's replica, polling the resolver session's record. Withdrawn: dropping (d) for a forward (lands on the wrong replica); a §12.2 or §7.2 home for the record (extra edit, §7.2 is 0100 territory).
- **MISTAKE: elicitation poll "not found".** The poll exists in mcptools_register.go (`deps.Interactions.Get` on resolverSessionID, `awaitPollInterval` ticker); the earlier challenge searched only elicitation.go. CODE-2 item 7's "no such poll", the "Elicitation resolution across replicas" ABSENT claim row, and the summary watch-out are false; filed as a separate finding.
- **INTERACTION_ALREADY_RESOLVED.** The resolve handler answers 409 `INTERACTION_ALREADY_RESOLVED`, a code absent from spec/docs, while §7.2/§15.1 state 404. The (d) row cites §7.2/§9.2 and restates no code; the divergence is a separate finding.
- **Unregistered fan-outs.** §12.4 and §12.6 omit `token:revocations` and `credential:denylist:events`; do not add them here; they belong to the summary's "Unregistered fan-outs" defect. Withdrawn for `lenny:session:terminate`: tenant-prefixing it; adding only the row; "Three" to "Four".
- **Rule 7 Edge cases.** Do not re-add "client retry is deduplicated by message `id`" in non-spec Edge cases; TEST-5's same-id retry stays true. Withdrawn: deleting the retry sentence; a dedup key for message send; a separate resolved-`inReplyTo` clause.
- **Rule 6 checks scope.** No delivery-time scope re-check in §7.2 **Delivery of held messages.** Rule 6 names only the three §7.2 **Admission.** checks; CODE-2 also running PreMessageDelivery is not a deviation. On a rule-5 receiver "rate-limit checks" means the tool's own (messagingRateLimit). Withdrawn: checks before every forward (double-counts); extending §7.2 **Admission.**; enumerating limits in rule 3.
- **Rule 3 names "the coordinator".** Rule 5 sends a non-holder receiver into rule 6, yet rule 3 names only the coordinator; the forward listener authenticates every request alike, so it was not filed.
- **Not-delivered predicate at two sites.** SPEC-2 (b) ("absent or unreadable, or carries no address") and rule 6 both state it; home is rule 6. No later entry records it fixed.
- **`X-Lenny-Calling-Session` spelled only in non-spec CODE-1.** Filed by the round-4 edit-sites lens; no ledger entry records the outcome.
- **"Channel register places N channels" sentences.** Spread across §28.5.x leads and §29 scenario steps; grep "channel register places" in spec/29 when a channel joins a boundary.
- **Hard-wrapped anchors.** SPEC-4 (a), SPEC-4 (e), and SPEC-5 (f) quote text that spans line breaks; a literal grep misses them; that is not a missing anchor.
- **Erasure and §5.2 predicate.** Whether §5.2's "terminal state" predicate still selects a member after erasure deletes the row is F-12.8.43 territory.
- **Out-of-scope polish.** §3's boundary list is non-exhaustive ("including"), so omitting inter-replica is polish. The spec states no forward-rate budget, so do not file forwarding load. SPEC-5 (d) states no wake cadence; the code's 25 ms poll scales with pending interactions but has no spec budget to breach.
- **SPEC-5 (f) withdrawn placements.** A new SPEC-10 (needs a checklist step), filing under SPEC-4 (spec/28 only), and relettering in file order.

### [spec.9.review-performance.1]
FACT: The off-holder holder read the rules require (rule 1) is a single REG-COORDLEASE GET per off-holder request through the shipped leasestore.Failover, which reads Redis and falls back to the session_leases Postgres row only when Redis is unavailable. It adds no etcd or Postgres write load at Tier 3. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:101-106, pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/leasestore.go:194-199
FACT: Failure modes traced against the staged rules. Postgres failover: the holder is still read from Redis, so forwarding continues; only the SPEC-6 (c) primary-terminal read can miss, and that drops an at-most-once release, which the CH-BINDRELEASE Degradation bullet already states. Redis reset: holder reads fall back to Postgres; the DLQ is Redis-only, so a not-delivered message gets inbox_unavailable (settled). Coordinator handoff: rule 5 makes the receiver take rule 6, so no worse than the shipped in-memory inbox. Round 9 found nothing in the performance lens.

### [f1.open-decisions-apply.msg-dedup-writer]
DECISION: Resolved the summary open decision "Which replica records the sender-supplied message id of a forwarded message?" as already staged (rung 1): the replica that performs the delivery records the §15.4 id, which is the coordinator on a delivered forward and the replica applying rule 6 on an undelivered one. No staged text was written; the entry was removed from the summary's `## Open decisions for human to make`. — BECAUSE §29.3 **Forwarding.** rule 1 forbids any serving-replica state write before the forward decision (spec-changes.md:225-226), the §15.4 dedup record is a Redis sorted-set write (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1671), and rule 4 exempts the coordinator only from §11.1, §11.5, and §4.8 `PreToolResult` (spec-changes.md:243-247) — ALTERNATIVES: an explicit clause in rule 4, rejected as unnecessary under the least-text rule; recording at the receiving replica before forwarding, rejected because rule 1 forbids it and it loses the message on an undelivered forward.
FACT: TEST-5 (non-spec-changes.md:273) expects a same-id retry to be deduplicated after the coordinator received the request, which holds only under this answer. This closes the Standing context OPEN "msg_dedup writer on a forward".

### [f1.open-decisions-apply.rule5-409-idempotency]
DECISION: Resolved the summary open decision "How is a `TARGET_NOT_READY` answer from a replica that lost the lease kept out of the idempotency cache?" as mark-and-recognise (rung 4). Wrote a CODE-1 item 8 bullet **Forwarded-request refusal** (non-spec-changes.md, after **Responses**): the helper's 409 on a forwarded request carries a gateway-internal mark of the implementor's choosing; a helper receiving a delivered response with the mark strips it and the forwarding replica does not store the response in the §11.5 cache. Added a TEST-1 item 4 case (a resume with an `Idempotency-Key` forwarded to a non-holder stores no row; an unmarked coordinator 409 is stored). Removed the summary entry; the section is now empty. No checklist change (no deliverable added or renamed). AUTHORITY: spec-changes.md:249 (rule 5 receiver "applies the not-delivered outcome of rule 6 itself"), spec-changes.md:315 (§11.5 does not store a rule 6 or rule 7 `TARGET_NOT_READY`), so accepting a 24-hour cache contradicts staged text; Standing context trap "SPEC-5 (e) withdrawn homes" and spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:291 (duplicates replay the cached response) rule out blanket recognition of every 409; pkg/gateway/middleware/idempotency/idempotency.go:459 (`maybePersist` skips only 5xx) shows no existing path.
WATCHOUT: The bullet requires the forwarding replica not to store the marked response but stages no middleware hook; the Deferred "Idempotency gate for SPEC-5 (e)" (`DoNotStore`) is the natural mechanism and remains unauthored. When it is staged, the mark's recognition calls the same gate.
FACT: This closes the Standing context OPEN "SPEC-5 (e) rule-5 409".

### [f1.impacts-apply.0097-gateway-egress-row]
DECISION: Added a 0097 row to the summary's `## Impacts on other proposals`: 0101 SPEC-7 (b) and 0097 SPEC-10 Edit 1 edit the same §13.2.4 Gateway-row Egress cell at separate anchors, both apply in either order, the second to land re-reads the row and `allow-gateway-egress`, and 0097's approval stands. No checklist change. AUTHORITY: proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.status.md:5,10 (Approved, approved-date 2026-10-24); 0097 spec-changes.md:273-277 (Edit 1 replacement keeps the CoreDNS lead and the external HTTPS tail); 0101 spec-changes.md:374-380 (insertion after the Token Service clause); spec/13_security-model.md:277 (both anchors present, no KMS peer yet); 0097 non-spec-changes.md:85,192-194 and 0101 non-spec-changes.md:147,165.
FACT: The log has no `## Retired` section, so this block sits at the end of `## Ledger`.

### [f1.open-decisions-apply.forward-no-deadline-wait]
DECISION: Resolved summary open decision OD1 (length of the no-deadline wait in the forward helper's unit test) as already staged (rung 1): the TEST-1 item 4 row keeps the 35-second coordinator delay, run in parallel for a message and an interrupt. No staged text was written; the OD1 entry was replaced in the summary's `## Open decisions for human to make` by "None.". No checklist change. AUTHORITY: non-spec-changes.md:235 (the staged 35-second row); non-spec-changes.md:232 (the only other timing rows pin the 2-second dial and handshake bound, so no other row runs past 30 s); cmd/lenny-test/timeouts.go:56 (`tierUnitTimeout` 1800 s per package); non-spec-changes.md:74 (CODE-1 item 8 **Client.** sets no `Timeout`). ALTERNATIVES: a 3-second wait, rejected because a reintroduced forward-only 30-second bound passes it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]

FACT: The pending `lenny/request_input` wait is process-local (`inputwait`) on the replica the pod's LNK-GWCONTROL connection reached, which need not be the lease holder. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md LNK-GWCONTROL row "One connection per pod process to one replica"; tests/claim-map.json "Input-wait resolution across replicas" surface `inputwait.go` process-local.
CORRECTS [Standing context Traps, "Matching inReplyTo is unevaluable on the serving replica"]: "The pending request_input wait is coordinator-process-only" is false; the wait is on the LNK-GWCONTROL replica. The second clause (never key a serving-side predicate on a match) stays true.
DECISION: SPEC-5 (c) keeps the forward to the coordinator and the fail-closed 409; only the false "held in the coordinating replica's process alone" reason was deleted (spec-changes.md edited, one clause). REC-1 input-wait row moves to ABSENT, R20 — BECAUSE plan R19 re-derivation moves the `inputwait` row to R20 and summary Non-goals keep cross-replica input-wait storage with R20 — ALTERNATIVES: store-and-wake for request_input (stages R20, breaks forward-first on a mixed batch); forwarding request_input itself (new long-blocking forward site).
DECISION: F-29.3.2 stays closed by CODE-2 and moved to TEST-2 item 2 — BECAUSE BUILD-GAPS F-29.3.2 says "this finding is the buffering location", which the forward plus DLQ hold close.
WATCHOUT: TEST-2 item 3 pins the LNK-GWCONTROL connection on the coordinator; do not rewrite it to pin the non-coordinator and assert resolution, which asserts R20 behaviour and fails until R20 lands. — EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md R19 re-derivation ("The claim register's `inputwait` row moves from R19 to R20").
DECISION: The REST message forward call site is `parseMessageBatch`, after decode and validation and before its `inReplyTo` resolve loop; the kind is per request, `kindMessageNoReplyTo` only when no message in the batch carries `inReplyTo` — BECAUSE parseMessageBatch resolves a matching reply locally before `restDelivery.route` runs, so a call in `route` re-delivers it, and a mixed batch must not reach the DLQ (rule 6) — ALTERNATIVES: per-message split (one response cannot be receipt and 409; forward replays the whole body).
WATCHOUT: `parseMessageBatch` decodes with a streaming `json.NewDecoder` over `http.MaxBytesReader` and keeps no bytes, so the implementor must read the body into a buffer, decode those bytes, and forward the same bytes (rule 2, TEST-1 item 6 byte-equality). The non-spec Edge-cases trap "a message handler reads the request body before routing, so the bytes it read are what it passes" describes the required code, not the current tree. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go parseMessageBatch; json_body.go jsonReader.
MISTAKE: CODE-2 item 7 ("add a poll if missing"), the ABSENT "Elicitation resolution across replicas" REC-1 row, and the summary toolapproval.go watch-out were built on an elicitation poll search that missed mcptools_register.go; all deleted this round, and D13 now reads "Tool approval and elicitation already work this way." — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go elicitation await (`time.NewTicker(awaitPollInterval)`, `deps.Interactions.Get(... resolverSessionID ...)`); mcptools.go `const awaitPollInterval = 25 * time.Millisecond`; tests/tier7a_load_local/elicitation_xreplica_wake_test.go.
CORRECTS [Standing context Open, UNVERIFIED "awaitPollInterval meaning"]: the elicitation wait ticks on `awaitPollInterval` (verified in mcptools_register.go).
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add an elicitation claim row or a conditional poll step; the contract holds in code, so no claim row is owed.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]

- DECISION: CODE-1 item 8 **Forwarded-request refusal** replaced in place by **Idempotency gate**: one per-request context gate (`idempotency.WithGate`, `idempotency.DoNotStore`), installed by the §11.5 middleware's ServeHTTP on the serving replica and by the forward listener on the receiver; `maybePersist` checks it first, releases the pending row, skip reason `forward_refused`; the listener turns a set gate into a no-store mark on the response, and the receiving helper strips it and calls DoNotStore — BECAUSE maybePersist stores every non-5xx, non-streamed response and the middleware wraps `/v1/sessions/{id}/resume` and `/mcp`, so the serving replica's own rule 6/7 409 was cached 24h; an /mcp tool handler owns no ResponseWriter, so a header-only mark cannot reach it, while the context gate does (mcp.Server handleToolCall uses r.Context()) — ALTERNATIVES: header mark only (misses MCP, leaks to unwrapped routes); keeping the mark and adding DoNotStore on recognition (leaves serving-replica 409s cached); 5xx answer; body-parsing every TARGET_NOT_READY (both withdrawn earlier).
- FACT: idempotency middleware stores every response that is neither streamed nor status>=500, with no decline hook; skip reasons are `streamed` and `server_error`, named only in Go doc comments — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/idempotency/idempotency.go maybePersist; pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics.go (skip-reason comment near IncIdempotencyCacheSkipped), gatewaymetrics_audit.go.
- WATCHOUT: DoNotStore must be called before the helper writes the 409 header on the receiver, because the listener adds the mark when the header is written; the staged text says "before it writes or returns the refusal" for that reason. Do not shorten it to "before it returns". — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Idempotency gate**.
- WATCHOUT: Do not call DoNotStore for kindMessageNoReplyTo not-delivered: that outcome answers a DLQ hold receipt, and leaving it uncached lets a same-key /mcp retry enqueue twice. The bullet keys on the **Responses** 409 set by pointer for this reason. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Responses**.
- USEFUL [review-log Deferred "Idempotency gate for SPEC-5 (e)"]: its staging text was the base of the bullet. This round discharges that Deferred and the matching Open "No staged code keeps a rule 6 or rule 7 TARGET_NOT_READY out of the §11.5 cache"; compaction can drop both. The skip reason is `forward_refused`, not the Deferred's `forward_failed`, because the gate also covers a delivered marked refusal.
- DECISION: forward bound comes from new flags `--request-timeout`/`LENNY_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` (30s) and `--upload-timeout`/`LENNY_UPLOAD_TIMEOUT` (300s), read only by the forward helper via context.WithTimeout on the request's context — BECAUSE no gateway code implements §11.3 `gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds` (http.Server sets only ReadHeaderTimeout), so "runs under r.Context()" left the forward unbounded after connect; code-best-practices requires an override for a default — ALTERNATIVES: hard-coded constants (no override); per-kind budget table (0100 text not landed, adds mechanism); Transport.ResponseHeaderTimeout or Client.Timeout (stalled body unbounded / ignores shorter ctx deadline).
- FACT: no requestTimeout/uploadTimeout wiring exists in pkg/ or cmd/; §11.3 lists Request timeout 30s and Upload timeout 300s as configurable — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go httpSrv construction (ReadHeaderTimeout only); spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.3 table.
- WATCHOUT: terminate and delete forwards are bounded by the 30s request timeout; a coordinator seal that runs longer answers the client 409 (outcome unknown, rule 7) while the coordinator completes the write. Legal under rule 7; do not "fix" it with a per-kind budget without a spec basis. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Client.**
- OPEN: §11.3 `gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds` and `gateway.uploadTimeoutSeconds` are implemented nowhere gateway-wide and the chart wires neither; candidate BUILD-GAPS finding outside 0101. Whoever owns §11.3 should decide whether CHART-1's flags later fold into a gateway-wide bound.
- DECISION: MCP sites return the tool error `TARGET_NOT_READY` with `currentState` for every outcome **Responses** answers with 409, the `playground_client_closed` best-effort cancel included — BECAUSE answering `accepted: true` would report a hint that never reached the coordinator — ALTERNATIVES: accepted:true for the best-effort cancel (rejected); patching the CODE-2 item 4 restatement (second home of **Responses**). CODE-2 item 4's restating bullets collapsed into one pointer carrying the batch clause.
- DECISION: MCP cancel forward site is the `lenny/cancel_session` handler after its row read and `session.Validate`, before `cancelSession` — BECAUSE cancelSession reads no row and opens BeginTerminal first — ALTERNATIVES: row read inside cancelSession (second read, forward inside the terminal claim). Closes the standing Open "cancelSession forward site"; compaction can drop it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go lenny/cancel_session handler and cancelSession.
- OPEN: non-spec-changes.md design overview "Its one wire field is the calling-session header" is arguably false since a no-store mark rides forward responses (pre-existing since round 1's mark bullet); a later round should decide whether the mark counts as a wire field and reword the sentence.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]

DECISION: The forward helper returns `outcomeLocal` without a lease read when the `coordinatesSession` guard holds (no lease store, no pod binder, or empty replica identity), and both share one method for that guard. BECAUSE the stock chart (redis.url empty) runs two gateways with no lease store, and Source Mode runs one; both coordinate every session by the existing guard. ALTERNATIVES: listing the three conditions in the bullet (second copy of a code predicate); calling coordinatesSession in place of the read (it folds read error and absent holder into false and hides the holder value the helper needs).
FACT: `coordinatesSession` returns true when `s.leaseStore == nil || s.podBinder == nil || s.replicaID == ""` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:88-91
DECISION: Listener and client take the gateway leaf through one `certreload.New` reloader (`GetCertificate` / `GetClientCertificate`) over the --adapter-tls-cert/key paths. BECAUSE §10.3 **For long-running sessions:** requires callback reload, and the leaf is 24h. ALTERNATIVES: restart on renewal (§10.3 forbids). CA-pool reload was deliberately not added; no finding raised it.
FACT: certreload API is `certreload.New(certFile, keyFile) (*Reloader, error)` with `GetCertificate` and `GetClientCertificate` methods; existing users are pkg/adapter/transport.go, cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go, policychain_seams.go — EVIDENCE: pkg/mtls/certreload/certreload.go:47,105,115
DECISION: S7 depends on proposal 0097 CODE-4 ("Gateway wiring: shared signer and key set"); reason given once in the summary 0097 row. BECAUSE gateway bearers are signed by a per-process KMS-envelope HMAC key (`jwt.NewKMSSigner(..., "boot")`), so a peer coordinator answers 401 to a forwarded gateway-minted bearer until 0097 lands — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:88. The summary "Order matters" list was left for G7.
DECISION: CHART-1 adds a render-time fail for `mtls.enabled: false`, `global.devMode: false`, and Redis configured (`$hasRedis`, `$hasRedisCluster`, `$useSentinel`, gateway-deployment.yaml:59-61). BECAUSE SPEC-7 NET-072 already refuses that gateway at startup, and the chart passes --adapter-tls-* only under `mtls.enabled` (gateway-deployment.yaml:575-596). Keyed on Redis so the existing no-Redis mtls.enabled=false helm-unittest cases keep rendering. ALTERNATIVES: mount the leaf independently of mtls.enabled (needs cert-manager in mesh mode, a second cert path); plaintext forward under a mesh (contradicts SPEC-7); treat mesh like dev mode (silent loss of the forward D5 prevents).
WATCHOUT: The tier-5 non-holder forwarding lane was deleted, not skipped. The Kind overlay runs `global.devMode: true` and `mtls.enabled: false` because turning mtls on breaks adapter dials (adapter serves plaintext, F-10.3.10), so no forwarder exists there. Forward coverage end to end is TEST-2's tier-4 two-subprocess lane with real certificates. Do not re-add a Kind forwarding lane until the overlay carries gateway cert material — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:28-43, :340
OPEN: TEST-6 tier-9 cases "Port 50054 refuses a connection from an agent pod and from lenny-ops" and the non-gateway-SAN case run against the same Kind overlay, where no listener runs, so the refusal passes vacuously; the SAN case also duplicates TEST-1 item 3. Logged as a separate finding by the design; not edited here.

With SPEC-7 NET-072's startup refusal and the CHART-1 render refusal, a non-dev mesh install with Redis is no longer possible; a staged edit should qualify that sentence (mesh mode only without Redis, or not at all outside dev mode).
FACT: CHART-1 heading and the summary deliverable-index line for CHART-1 still read "Port, advertise address, listener, and NetworkPolicy self-peer"; they are titles and were left; checklist S4's enumeration was extended with "render refusal".

### [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: Added TEST-7 (tier-11 re-pins of spec_28_register_writers_test.go, off_holder_matrix_stated_outcome_test.go, redis_key_prefix_registry_test.go, and the rediskeys.go package comment) on a new checklist step S3 · test after the spec steps; S1 and S2 now run tier 0 only; old S3..S15 became S4..S16 — BECAUSE one lane per step and no test lands in a spec commit, so the first non-spec step is the earliest point tier 11 can be green — ALTERNATIVES: bundling into S1 (breaks one-lane); folding into REC-1's currency-test Trap (those are record-currency tests updated at code steps).
DECISION: The register-writers stale-exemption self-test passes `underDeclaredLinkEntries` an exemption set (new parameter) exempting the twice-referred link, in place of a synthetic link identifier — BECAUSE `referringChannelRows` iterates only declared links, so a synthetic identifier needs a mutated link table, while exempting an existing declared link exercises the exempt-and-referred branch with no table mutation — ALTERNATIVES: synthetic identifier (needs a link-row insertion helper registerTable lacks; `withCell` only edits cells).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

FACT: The `lenny/request_input` wait registers in the process-local `inputwait.Registry` of the replica that serves the platform tool call, which is the replica the pod's `LNK-GWCONTROL` connection reached through the gateway ClusterIP. That replica is independent of the `REG-COORDLEASE` holder. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:108 (LNK-GWCONTROL row); pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go ConnectGateway; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1024 `deps.InputWaits.Register`; tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/harness.go:310 (one registry per harness replica).
FACT: The REST message handler resolves a matching `inReplyTo` locally inside `parseMessageBatch` (messages_delivery.go:63-72), before `deliverMessageBatch` and `restDelivery.route`. A forward placed in `route` therefore runs after a local resolve, and the replayed batch makes the coordinator deliver the already-resolved reply a second time as an ordinary threaded message. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages.go:190-200, messages_delivery.go:63-72.
FACT: Held-message delivery never runs `inputWaits.Resolve`, so a reply held in the DLQ reaches the runtime as an ordinary message and does not resolve the call it answers. — EVIDENCE: no `inputWaits` reference in pkg/gateway/sessionserver/held_delivery.go.
FACT: Forward-first is not a pre-R20 regression. Today a reply resolves when the serving replica holds the wait. After CODE-2 it resolves when the coordinator holds the wait. Each case has probability 1/N, and with two replicas the two cases are complementary halves.
DECISION: Finding 13. Delete only the false "because ... coordinating replica's process alone" clause in SPEC-5 (c), keep the forward and the fail-closed 409, rewrite D10's reason, move the claim row "Input-wait resolution across replicas" to R20, and make TEST-2 item 3 pin the `LNK-GWCONTROL` connection to the coordinator. F-29.3.2 stays resolved by CODE-2 — BECAUSE F-29.3.2 is explicitly the buffering location ("this finding is the buffering location", BUILD-GAPS.md:50148), which the forward and the DLQ hold close. Cross-replica wait resolution is 6c.4, which the plan assigns to R20 (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:1695, :1712), and the summary already makes it a non-goal. — ALTERNATIVES: stating store-and-wake for request_input in SPEC-5 (c) (that stages R20's mechanism against the proposal's own non-goal, and R20's spec step states it together with its request-input interaction kind); forwarding request_input to the session's coordinator (that adds a long-blocking forward site and leaves await_children's child-wait read cross-replica broken); un-recording F-29.3.2 (it misreads the finding's scope).
DECISION: Finding 15. Decide the forward kind per request. `kindMessageNoReplyTo` applies only when no message in the batch carries `inReplyTo`. Any other message request gets 409 and holds nothing, which is rule 6's "every other request". Move the REST call into `parseMessageBatch`, ahead of its resolve loop. No spec edit — BECAUSE rule 6 already reads at the request level ("Every other request ... fails closed"). — ALTERNATIVES: per-message split, holding the plain messages and refusing the replies (one response cannot carry both, and a forward replays the whole body); rewording rule 6 to say "a request none of whose messages" (correct, but it triggers a spec recheck for wording that already resolves).
WATCHOUT: Rule 6 says "for a message carrying no `inReplyTo`". A spec-lane reviewer may file it as ambiguous for a REST batch. CODE-2 item 4 holds the application, and the "every other request" sentence is the anchor. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 (a) rule 6.
DECISION: Finding 3. Delete CODE-2 item 7, its Targets bullet, the REC-1 elicitation row, the summary watch-out, the "drives CODE-2 item 7" sentence, and `elicitation.go` in Files touched. Rewrite D13's second sentence to name elicitation alongside tool approval — BECAUSE the poll exists and the cross-replica wake is already pinned by tests/tier7a_load_local/elicitation_xreplica_wake_test.go. A claim row is owed only for a contract that does not hold in code. — ALTERNATIVES: a WIRED elicitation row (extra text that the rules do not require).
CORRECTS [standing context "'Matching inReplyTo' is unevaluable on the serving replica"]: "The pending request_input wait is coordinator-process-only" is false. The wait is on the replica the pod's `LNK-GWCONTROL` connection reached, which may be neither the serving replica nor the coordinator. The presence predicate stands for that reason.
CORRECTS [standing context UNVERIFIED "awaitPollInterval meaning"]: both entries are true. `awaitPollInterval` (mcptools.go:1865, 25 ms) ticks the await_children poll (mcptools_register.go:701) and the elicitation wait's `deps.Interactions.Get` poll (mcptools_register.go:1448).
USEFUL [standing context "MISTAKE: elicitation poll 'not found'"]: it located the poll at once.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Idempotency gate is one context gate in pkg/gateway/middleware/idempotency (`WithGate`, `DoNotStore`), installed by `ServeHTTP` and by the forward listener; the core calls `DoNotStore` for every outcome CODE-1 item 8 **Responses** answers with 409; the forward listener turns a set gate into the no-store mark, so the rule-5 receiver case works on `/mcp` too — BECAUSE a response-header mark alone cannot reach a JSON-RPC tool error (the tool handler owns no writer) and leaks to clients on paths the middleware does not wrap; the gate reaches MCP tool handlers because mcp.Server's POST path uses `r.Context()` (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcp/mcp.go:372) — ALTERNATIVES: header mark recognised by the middleware (leaks, MCP-blind); the round-1 "mark of the implementor's choosing" set by the helper (MCP receiver has no writer); body-parsing every 409 (catches §15.1 pre-running).
CORRECTS [standing Traps "SPEC-5 (e) withdrawn homes"]: "the gate is on the serving replica, the mark on the coordinator" — after G2 the gate is installed on both the serving replica (idempotency `ServeHTTP`) and the forward listener; the do-not-merge-with-`Forwarded(ctx)` point still holds.
USEFUL [standing Deferred "Idempotency gate for SPEC-5 (e)"]: the derived remedy was the base of the G2 design; skip reason renamed `forward_refused`. The Deferred and the standing Open "cancelSession forward site" are discharged once G2 lands.
DECISION: forward deadline is the §11.3 Request timeout (Upload timeout for upload-to-session), carried by new flags `--request-timeout` and `--upload-timeout`, applied with `context.WithTimeout` on the request's context — BECAUSE standing context already reads rule 8's deadline as `gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds`, nothing in the tree implements it (http.Server sets only ReadHeaderTimeout, cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:976-980; no requestTimeout in pkg/ or cmd/), and code-best-practices.md:71 bars an unoverridable constant — ALTERNATIVES: hard-coded 30s (violates :71); per-kind terminal-pipeline bound for terminate (0100 not landed, adds a kind table); ResponseHeaderTimeout (same problem, splits rule 8).
WATCHOUT: with the 30s bound, an off-holder terminate whose coordinator-side seal runs longer than 30s answers 409 (rule 7 unknown outcome) while the same terminate on the holder answers the sealed row, because no on-holder handler applies §11.3 Request timeout yet. Rule 7 makes this legal; a reviewer may file it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go cancelSession doc ("returns the row the hook returned"); spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:208.
OPEN: §11.3 Request timeout and Upload timeout (`gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds`, `gateway.uploadTimeoutSeconds`) are implemented nowhere in the gateway and absent from charts/lenny/values.yaml; candidate BUILD-GAPS finding outside 0101. G2's flags are read only by the forward helper.
DECISION: MCP forward failure answers the tool error TARGET_NOT_READY with currentState for every outcome **Responses** answers with 409, the `playground_client_closed` best-effort cancel included — BECAUSE rule 6/7 admit no exception and §27.6 (spec/27_web-playground.md:205) states no `accepted:true` result; the accepted:true branches are code-only — ALTERNATIVES: accepted:true for best-effort (reports success for an effect that did not reach the coordinator; an exception clause).
FACT: the `lenny/cancel_session` handler does the row read and `session.Validate`; `cancelSession` reads no row and opens `BeginTerminal` first, so the forward belongs in the handler before the `cancelSession` call — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:394-414, :442-447.
WATCHOUT: non-spec-changes.md design line "Its one wire field is the calling-session header" was already false once round 1 added the forwarded-refusal mark; G2 keeps the mark, so it stays false. Separate finding, not fixed in G2.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]

DECISION: no-lease-store gate in the helper — CODE-1 item 8 **Lease read.** returns `outcomeLocal` under the guard that opens `coordinatesSession`, shared as one method — BECAUSE a stock chart render (redis.url empty by default) and Source Mode wire no lease store, and today `coordinatesSession` treats that as "coordinates everything" — ALTERNATIVES: restating the guard's three conditions in the bullet (second copy of a code predicate that drifts).
FACT: the stock chart has no Redis: values.yaml `redis.url: ""`, so `$hasRedis` is false in gateway-deployment.yaml and the gateway runs with no lease store at 2 replicas. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:498, :2153; cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:208; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:88-91.
DECISION: cert rotation — the listener and the client take `GetCertificate`/`GetClientCertificate` from one `certreload.New(adapterTLSCert, adapterTLSKey)` reloader — BECAUSE §10.3 **For long-running sessions:** requires the callbacks and every other gateway mTLS surface already uses certreload — ALTERNATIVES: none viable; a static load breaks forwarding about 24h after start.
FACT: certreload users: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:211, policychain_seams.go:100, pkg/adapter/transport.go:102/148. The CA pools (ClientCAs/RootCAs) are still read once; CA rotation via the previousCA trust bundle is a separate question nobody has filed.
DECISION: 0097 dependency — S7 (CODE-2) depends on 0097 CODE-4 (0097 checklist S16, gateway wiring); the reason lives in the summary 0097 interaction row; the summary "Order matters" bullet names it — BECAUSE before 0097 each gateway signs with a per-process key (jwt.NewKMSSigner "boot"), so a coordinator 401s a forwarded bearer a peer minted, which regresses off-holder terminate/delete that today write locally — ALTERNATIVES: forwarding a peer-asserted principal (D5 and rule 3 forbid it); depending on 0097 CODE-5 too (Token Service bearers verify on no gateway today, so they cannot regress).
DECISION: mesh mode — CHART-1 adds a render-time `fail` in gateway-deployment.yaml when mtls.enabled is false, global.devMode is false, and Redis is configured (`$hasRedis`, `$hasRedisCluster`, or `$useSentinel`); D5 gains one clause naming the consequence — BECAUSE staged SPEC-7 NET-072 already makes that gateway refuse to start, and the chart has a fail-guard pattern (gateway-deployment.yaml:94-144) — ALTERNATIVES: mounting the gateway leaf independently of mtls.enabled (mtls-pki.yaml renders no Secret when mtls.enabled=false and mesh installs set certmanager.enabled=false); a plaintext mesh-mode forward (needs a SPEC-7 change and a second listener mode, and the listener trusts `X-Lenny-Calling-Session` only because peers are verified gateways).
WATCHOUT: existing helm-unittest cases set mtls.enabled=false with no Redis and no devMode; the guard must key on Redis or they break. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/tests/gateway-deployment_test.yaml:231-240, :288-297.
DEFERRED [proposals/0101_new_gateway-replicas-have-no-way-to-ask-the-replica/0101_new_gateway-replicas-have-no-way-to-ask-the-replica.spec-changes.md]: §10.3 **Certificate authority:** says "a service mesh (Istio/Linkerd) is an optional alternative" to cert-manager. Under SPEC-7 NET-072 a non-dev gateway with Redis needs the cert-manager gateway leaf, so a mesh cannot replace the PKI for a production install. SPEC-7 should amend that sentence, or the owner should carve mesh mode out of the refusal.
OPEN: owner — is dropping non-dev service-mesh installs (mtls.enabled=false with Redis) acceptable, as SPEC-7 implies? The values.yaml mtls comment (charts/lenny/values.yaml:1992-1994) and mtls-pki.yaml:29-30 still advertise the mode.
DECISION: Kind lane — delete the TEST-6 tier-5 non-holder lane, and re-annotate the remaining lifecycle_drain sentence to 12.8 and 28.5.7 — BECAUSE the Kind overlay runs devMode=true with mtls.enabled=false (adapter serving certs are still unstamped), so no listener or forwarder runs there, and TEST-2's tier-4 two-subprocess lane with real certificates already drives message, resume, and terminate through the forward — ALTERNATIVES: turning mtls on in the overlay (breaks adapter dials, e2e-values.yaml:29-43); a chart knob that mounts a gateway leaf for the forward alone (second cert path, hair).
WATCHOUT: Kind runs 2 gateway replicas by default (values.yaml gateway.replicas: 2; the overlay does not override it), and after CODE-2 every off-holder forward in Kind takes the not-delivered outcome. A tier-5 test that reaches the non-holder for terminate/delete gets 409, where today the write is local. kubectl port-forward to the Service pins one pod, which hides this. EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:43, :340; tests/tier5_e2e_kind/ha_streaming_replica_test.go:314.
OPEN: separate finding for a later round — TEST-6 tier-9 cases "Port 50054 refuses a connection from an agent pod and from lenny-ops" and the non-gateway-SAN case need an in-cluster listener, which the Kind overlay does not run (dev mode, no certs), so the refusal passes vacuously. The SAN case also duplicates TEST-1 item 3.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: The tier-11 re-pins become a new deliverable TEST-7 (appended after TEST-6) on a new test-lane checklist step S3, after both spec steps, with Depends on S1, S2. Later steps renumber S3..S15 to S4..S16. REC-1, CHART-1, and DOCS-1 add S3 to Depends on because they run tier 11. S1 and S2 drop tier 11 — BECAUSE implement-proposal allows one lane per step ("a step naming both a spec deliverable and a non-spec one has none"), so the re-pins cannot ride S1's commit. A test step between S1 and S2 would be an interleave that does not qualify. Spec-lane steps run no tests (runSpecStep in .claude/workflows/implement-proposal-build.js), so S3 is where tier 11 first runs — ALTERNATIVES: bundling the tests into S1 (no handler for the step); folding the re-pins into REC-1 at S3 (no renumbering, but it makes a records deliverable a grab bag of spec-text pins); deleting the three tests (they pin live rules: the §28.3 link threshold, the off-holder matrix, and the §12.4 registry).
FACT: Spec-lane steps apply text, verify it, and commit. They run no tier. Tier failures caused by a spec step therefore surface at the next non-spec step's final gate — EVIDENCE: .claude/workflows/implement-proposal-build.js:1807-1827, .claude/skills/implement-proposal/SKILL.md:20
WATCHOUT: Keep `unreferencedLinkEntries` in spec_28_register_writers_test.go, emptied rather than deleted, because the §28.3 rule still admits "a connection the specification states and no channel is carried on yet". ### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The REST http.Server sets only ReadHeaderTimeout (cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:976-980); `gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds` (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.3 table) has no flag, chart value, or per-request context deadline in the gateway. So r.Context() carries no deadline, and "the request runs under r.Context() (rule 8)" in CODE-1 item 8 leaves a post-GotConn forward unbounded. — EVIDENCE: grep requestTimeoutSeconds finds only spec/11 and the bootstrap Job values.
FACT: §15.4 message-id dedup (msg_dedup sorted set, DUPLICATE_MESSAGE_ID) has no pkg/ implementation (only errorclassify and the adapter proto enum), no claim-register row, and no BUILD-GAPS entry; TEST-5's "a client retry with the same message id is deduplicated" asserts it with no CODE item staging it. — EVIDENCE: grep msg_dedup/DUPLICATE_MESSAGE_ID in pkg/ cmd/ scripts/seed-claim-register.py BUILD-GAPS.md.
FACT: sessionserver's lease store is the failover store (cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:208-221), so the forward helper's lease read survives a Redis outage via session_leases; no finding there.
WATCHOUT: The idempotency DoNotStore gate (Standing context Deferred "Idempotency gate for SPEC-5 (e)") is still unstaged; the round-1 non-spec **Forwarded-request refusal** bullet requires non-storage with no middleware hook, and pkg/gateway/middleware/idempotency/idempotency.go:432-475 maybePersist skips only streamed and 5xx. Filed again this round under reliability (keyed retry replays a 409 for 24h).
USEFUL [Standing context "Idempotency middleware behaviour"]: accurate; saved re-deriving maybePersist.

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]

- FACT: every existing gateway mTLS dial or listener loads its leaf through `pkg/mtls/certreload` (GetCertificate/GetClientCertificate), and §10.3 requires it ("must use filesystem-watching TLS configuration"). Gateway leaves are 24h TTL. A static `tls.Config{Certificates: ...}` stops working at about 24h of process uptime. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:329, :357; pkg/adapter/transport.go:145-152; cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:207-224
- WATCHOUT: CODE-1 items 6 and 8 stage static `Certificates` on the forward listener and client. Any rewrite has to use certreload on both ends. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:57, :73
- FACT: the idempotency middleware persists every non-5xx, non-streamed response and has no decline hook, so a serving replica's own rule 6/7 409 on an Idempotency-Key resume is cached for 24h unless code adds a decline. The round-7 "forwarded-request refusal" bullet covers only a delivered response from a non-holder receiver. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/idempotency/idempotency.go:419-470; non-spec-changes.md:76
- FACT: until 0097 lands, gateway-minted bearers verify only on the minting process (per-process HS256 key in jwt.NewKMSSigner, RotatingVerifier over the same key), so the coordinator's re-authentication in rule 3 refuses them with 401. 0097 sits on its own impl branch and is not merged into this tree. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:88, :118; proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/*problem-statement.md
- FACT: the NetworkPolicy surface is clean. No existing policy admits gateway pods on a port that would overlap 50054, and the gateway leaf SAN appears only on lenny-gateway-tls. The docs list gateway policies only in docs/operator-guide/security.md:316, which DOCS-1 covers. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:640-699; charts/lenny/templates/mtls-pki.yaml:17-22

### [non-spec.2.fix-G4.1]

FACT: The gateway persists `input_required` and `resuming` as distinct session-row values, and `forwardStates` has to list them by literal value. §10.1.1 Primary renews the lease in both states. EVIDENCE: pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:118 StateInputRequired, :143 StateResuming; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go:312 `r.State = session.StateResuming`; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:28.
DECISION: CODE-2 item 2 lists, for terminate and delete together, `starting`, `running`, `input_required`, `resume_pending`, `resuming`, `awaiting_client_action`, and `suspended` with a pod. Interrupt is `running` and `input_required`. Upload stays `running`. The item carries one trap sentence about the literal lookup — BECAUSE the §29.3 rows use the §15.1 visible-state vocabulary (`input_required` is the §7.2 sub-state of `running`, and `resuming` is internal-only), so mapping those states to row values is code-only — ALTERNATIVES: separate terminate and delete lists with a "resuming once 0100 lands" clause (0101 lands after 0100's CODE-7 item 4 admits resuming for terminate); a single lease predicate (it would forward suspended messages and podless resume); §29.3 row edits (the rows stay true); `input_required` for upload (EndpointUpload admits only running and refuses before the forward point).
DECISION: The per-site state qualifiers in CODE-2 item 1 are deleted, which makes item 2 the one home for the gated states — BECAUSE the item 1 "for `running`" copy at handleInterrupt contradicted the widened list.
WATCHOUT: TEST-2 item 5 says "termination sequence", and it must not go back to "runs its seal". A `resuming` termination skips the live seal under §7.2 snapshot-close step 2. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-2 item 5.
DECISION: The "This closes F-..." sentences are deleted from TEST-2 items 1, 2, 4, and 5. The REC-1 **BUILD-GAPS.md.** list is the only statement of when each finding closes — BECAUSE TEST-2 item 1 claimed S9 closes F-7.2.38 while REC-1 ties that closure to TEST-3, and recording it at S9 would break TestBlockedSkipsNameOpenFindings_spec_7_2 — ALTERNATIVES: aligning the copies to REC-1 (it keeps two homes that would drift apart again).

### [non-spec.2.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: parseMessageBatch buffers the body (jsonReader, bounded by MaxJSONBodyBytes), decodes from the bytes, and passes the same bytes to forwardToHolder; stated once at the CODE-2 item 1 bullet — BECAUSE the forward kind and the DLQ hold need the decoded batch, so the unread-body option of CODE-1 item 8 **Outbound request** cannot apply, and parseMessageBatch is the only CODE-2 site that decodes before its forward point — ALTERNATIVES: re-marshal `req` (breaks TEST-1 item 6 byte-equality, drops unknown fields); forward unread r.Body before decoding (kind unknown); general rule in CODE-1 item 8 (a predicate with one member).
WATCHOUT: do not move the message forward into restDelivery.route or after the inReplyTo resolve loop to avoid buffering; a reply resolved locally would be forwarded and delivered again — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:30-36 (streaming decode over jsonReader, no raw bytes kept), :64-67 (s.inputWaits.Resolve)
FACT: jsonReader returns http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, MaxJSONBodyBytes) (1 MiB) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/json_body.go:17-28
DECISION: TEST-2 item 3 gains a tier-1 case (off-holder server, fake forwarder, inputwait registry holding a pending call for a session in `input_required`): the inReplyTo message is forwarded byte-equal and the wait stays pending — BECAUSE the tier-4 case has the pod connection on the coordinator, so the serving replica's registry is empty and a post-loop forward passes it; the pending wait is the discriminating assertion and the byte-equality also pins the buffering — ALTERNATIVES: a spy on Resolve (ties the test to a method name; SPEC-5 (c)'s outcome is the wait state); a separate TEST-1 case (duplicates setup).

### [non-spec.2.fix-G6.1]

DECISION: the message kinds forward under the request's context with no helper-imposed bound. Upload-to-session keeps `--upload-timeout`, and every other kind keeps `--request-timeout` (CODE-1 item 8 **Client.**) — BECAUSE the coordinator's deliverNow passes r.Context() to executor.Send, which waits for the agent turn's `response`. The local path has no deadline, so a 30s bound answered 409 for a message the runtime had already received, and its cancellation cut the coordinator's turn — ALTERNATIVES: request timeout plus the §7.2 delivery timeout (still cuts any turn over 60s, because the coordinator code waits for the whole turn and not for a consumption ack); a per-kind budget table (more mechanism, and no spec value exists for a message).
FACT: on the coordinator a message forward's response waits for the whole agent turn. The forwarder cancelling also cancels the coordinator's r.Context() mid-turn, and Send then leaves the turn to the reader — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go deliverNow (`ctx := d.r.Context()`, `s.executor.Send(ctx, ...)`); pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go Send doc; podstream.go runTurn/awaitReply.
CORRECTS [standing Settled "Rule 8 deadline."]: `gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds` (30s) bounds every non-message, non-upload forward. Upload uses the 300s upload timeout. The message kinds run under the client request's context with no helper bound. Rule 8 ("the client request's deadline") stays true without a spec edit.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 and non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 forward-bound DECISIONs]: those entries applied the 30s bound to all kinds, and the message kinds are now exempt. The terminate/delete WATCHOUT (seal over 30s gives 409) still holds.
WATCHOUT: do not put a bound back on the message kinds to "fix" an unbounded forward. Over 30s the forward answers 409 for a delivered message, and a client retrying without a message `id` delivers twice. TEST-1 item 4's message row ("answered after both timeouts returns the coordinator's receipt with no 409") discriminates — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Client.**, TEST-1 item 4.
DECISION: the keyed resume and keyed `/mcp` `lenny/cancel_session` no-store case moves to TEST-2 item 10 (S9, after CODE-2). TEST-1 item 4 keeps a helper-level case through test handlers at `/v1/sessions/{id}/resume` (forwardToHolder) and `/mcp` (ForwardToolCall) — BECAUSE those forward sites do not exist until CODE-2. At S7, resumeHeldPodRequest still writes its own cached 409 — ALTERNATIVES: making S7 depend on S8 (puts the R18 tests after R19's code).
FACT: tier-2 tests under cmd/ use `//go:build component`, and none exists yet in cmd/lenny-gateway. cmd/lenny-ops/eventmetrics_stream_test.go is the precedent. TEST-1 item 2's Postgres round-trip is therefore tier 2, in its own component-tagged file, and S7 runs tiers 0, 1, 2, 3 — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/eventmetrics_stream_test.go:3; cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage_quota_integration_test.go:3 (`integration`).

CORRECTS [the review-log OPENs at lines 303 and 390 on TEST-6 vacuous refusal]: closed by this edit.

DECISION: mesh-mode posture — keep D5 and the CHART-1 render refusal; add SPEC-7 (c), which replaces "a service mesh (Istio/Linkerd) is an optional alternative." in §10.3 **Certificate authority:** with a pointer qualifying it "wherever NET-072 below does not require the gateway replica certificate"; add one CHART-1 bullet that makes three chart comments cite the render refusal and NET-072: the values.yaml `mtls.enabled` comment, the mtls-pki.yaml header, and the gateway-deployment.yaml `/etc/lenny/mtls/` mount comment — BECAUSE NET-072's startup refusal is the root, so §10.3 contradicts itself until the CA sentence points at it, and those three comments say without condition that a mesh install sets mtls.enabled=false — ALTERNATIVES: carve mesh mode out of NET-072 (the forward listener trusts `X-Lenny-Calling-Session` only because NET-072 verifies the peer is a gateway, and a mesh's identity is invisible to the app; rejected in [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]); retire mesh mode across spec and chart (an owner-level change outside this proposal, which also affects the agent-pod and Token Service links); restate the predicate in every comment (the predicate's home is the CHART-1 refusal bullet and NET-072).

Do not refile them.

S11 drops "and the REC-1 entry that names CODE-4" and its S4 dependency, and S12 gains S4 — BECAUSE CODE-4 keeps the adminrouter.go path, so the row's surface stays true across the rename and S11 touches no REC-1 row; S12 flips the drain row S4 lands, so it needs S4 directly once S11 no longer carries it — ALTERNATIVES: naming a new surface value after CODE-4 (none exists, the path is unchanged); keeping S11's S4 dependency (harmless but unexplained once S11 no longer touches REC-1).
