### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: CODE-2 item 2 lists persisted row values: terminate and delete share one list that adds `input_required` and `resuming`, interrupt adds `input_required`, and upload stays `running`. One trap sentence says the lookup matches the row value literally. CODE-2 item 1 drops its per-site "for <state>" qualifiers, so item 2 is the only statement of the gated states. BECAUSE §10.1.1 says the lease is renewed in `input_required` and `resuming`. forwardStates matches row values, and the code persists both values (session.go StateInputRequired; resume_driver.go `r.State = session.StateResuming`). 0101 sequences after 0100, and 0100's CODE-7 item 4 adds StateResuming to EndpointTerminate, so terminate and delete need no "once 0100 lands" conditional. ALTERNATIVES: separate terminate and delete lists, rejected because after 0100 an off-holder terminate in `resuming` would write locally; a single §10.1.1 lease predicate for every kind, rejected because the message and resume kinds deliberately forward fewer states (suspended messages stay local, awaiting_client_action resume is unforwarded).
DECISION: upload-to-session keeps `running` only. BECAUSE EndpointUpload admits only StateRunning, gated on midSessionUpload, and the §15.1 check runs before the forward point, so an `input_required` upload is refused locally with no write. EVIDENCE: pkg/api/v1/session/session.go EndpointUpload; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session.go session.Validate call.
FACT: the §29.3 terminate, delete, and interrupt rows name §15.1 externally visible states. `running` there includes the §7.2 sub-state `input_required`, and §15.1 terminate and interrupt also list only `running`. `resuming` is §15.1 internal-only. The mapping to persisted row values therefore belongs only in non-spec CODE-2 item 2, and spec-changes "rows are unchanged" and the tree §29.3 rows stay true. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:213; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:648-653; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:28.
WATCHOUT: TEST-2 item 5 said the coordinator "runs its seal". In `resuming` the §7.2 snapshot-close sequence skips the live seal (step 2), so the item now says "runs its termination sequence". Do not restore "seal". EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:217-221.
DECISION: delete the four "This closes F-..." sentences in TEST-2 items 1, 2, 4, and 5. BECAUSE the REC-1 **BUILD-GAPS.md.** list is the one home for when each finding closes, and the TEST-2 copies already disagreed with it on F-7.2.38. ALTERNATIVES: aligning the copies to REC-1, rejected because it keeps two homes.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: parseMessageBatch reads the body into bytes through `jsonReader` (MaxJSONBodyBytes), decodes from those bytes, and passes them to `forwardToHolder`; stated as one added sentence on the CODE-2 item 1 parseMessageBatch bullet; CODE-1 item 8 **Outbound request** stays the contract's home unchanged — BECAUSE the forward kind (kindMessageNoReplyTo or not) needs the decoded batch, so the forward cannot take the unread body, and the contract already offers "the bytes it already read" — ALTERNATIVES: re-marshal `req` (breaks TEST-1 item 6 byte-equality, drops unknown fields); forward the raw body before decoding and decode only on not-delivered (body may be partly consumed by the transport, and the DLQ hold needs the decoded batch); a general "a site that decodes first buffers" rule in CODE-1 item 8 (parseMessageBatch is the only site that decodes before its forward point, so a general rule is a predicate with one member).
FACT: Of the CODE-2 item 1 sites, only parseMessageBatch decodes the body before its forward point. upload_to_session.go runs session.Validate before parseUploadToSession, so its forward takes the unread r.Body; handleTerminalWrite, handleInterrupt, and resumeHeldPodRequest read no body before the forward — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:30-36, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session.go:93-102
DECISION: TEST-2 item 3 gains one tier-1 case: off-holder, `input_required`, inputwait registry holding a pending call for the `inReplyTo` id, fake forwarder; the request is forwarded byte-equal, and the wait stays pending (the discriminating assertion; a forward after the resolve loop fails it) — BECAUSE the existing tier-4 case puts the pod connection on the coordinator, so the serving replica's registry is empty and a post-loop forward passes — ALTERNATIVES: a separate TEST-1 case for buffering (the byte-equal assertion in the same case pins it with no new item).
WATCHOUT: Do not "fix" this by moving the forward into `restDelivery.route` or after the resolve loop; CODE-2 item 1's second sentence states why — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:64-67

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G6.1]

DECISION: the forward adds no deadline of its own; the outbound request runs on the caller's request context (r.Context(), or ForwardToolCall's ctx), and the `--request-timeout`/`--upload-timeout` flags are deleted — BECAUSE rule 8 says "the client request's deadline", and the same request on the holder runs executor.Send on r.Context() with no deadline, so a forward-only bound is a deadline only off-holder requests carry; a 30s bound cuts message forwards while the coordinator waits for the agent's turn (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go deliverNow `ctx := d.r.Context()`; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go Send doc "waits for the reader to hand it the turn's `response`"), and the terminate-seal watchout of round 1 is the same defect for another kind. This differs in kind from round 1: it deletes the forward-local bound and moves the deadline to its owner (whatever deadline the gateway puts on the client request) instead of narrowing it per kind — ALTERNATIVES: exempting message kinds from the 30s bound (narrowing a failed claim; terminate still cut; per-kind table is hair); request timeout + §7.2 delivery timeout for messages (Send still waits for the whole turn, so 60s still cuts; adds a deadline the holder path lacks); detaching the coordinator's delivery from the forwarder's cancellation with WithoutCancel (breaks holder parity, new mechanism).
WATCHOUT: a reviewer will re-file round 1's "the forward has no deadline after connect". Answer: a hung coordinator holds the serving goroutine until the client disconnects, exactly as a hung pod holds the holder's handler. The gap is that §11.3 Request timeout (`gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds`) is enforced nowhere in the gateway (http.Server sets only ReadHeaderTimeout, cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go), which is pre-existing and affects both paths alike. A gateway-wide fix must also reconcile with PodExecutor.Send waiting for the full turn, where §7.2 path 2 (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md path 2) says the receipt is `delivered` after confirmed stdin consumption within the delivery timeout. Do not reintroduce a forward-only bound — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go Send; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.3 table.
OPEN: §11.3 Request/Upload timeout unenforced gateway-wide plus Send blocking on the full agent turn (divergence from §7.2 path 2 "delivered only after confirmed stdin consumption") is a candidate BUILD-GAPS finding outside 0101.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1 fix-design deadline DECISIONs, review-log ledger lines "forward bound comes from new flags `--request-timeout`..." and "forward deadline is the §11.3 Request timeout ..."]: superseded; the forward carries the caller's request context and no flag-driven bound. The paired WATCHOUTs on terminate/delete 409 after a 30s seal are obsolete with them.
CORRECTS [Standing context **Rule 8 deadline.**]: as a spec reading rule 8's deadline is the §11.3 Request timeout, but nothing in the gateway enforces it, and the forward helper does not either; do not read the entry as "every forward is cut at 30s".
DECISION: TEST-1 item 4's §11.5 no-store bullet tests the gate at the two CODE-1 entry points (forwardToHolder, ForwardToolCall) driven from test handlers; the keyed resume and keyed `/mcp` cancel_session assertions move into TEST-2 items 4 and 9 — BECAUSE those forward sites are created by CODE-2 (S8), so the S7 test cannot pass at S7 (resumeHeldPodRequest still writes its own 409, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go) — ALTERNATIVES: making S7 depend on S8 (TEST-1 then lands after a later-numbered step and stops gating CODE-1's own helper).
FACT: container-backed tests under cmd/ carry `//go:build component` (tier 2) or `integration` (tier 4): cmd/lenny-ops/eventmetrics_stream_test.go, cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage_quota_integration_test.go. TEST-1 item 2's real-Postgres round-trip is tier 2.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G7.1]

DECISION: TEST-6 Tier 9 replaces the listener-dependent cases with a probe pair against one gateway pod IP on 50054: a `lenny.dev/component: gateway` probe must not time out (curl exit 7 passes, because the Kind install runs no forward listener), and a `lenny-agents` probe and an `app: lenny-ops` probe must time out (exit 28). The SAN case is deleted (TEST-1 item 3 already rejects a different-DNS-SAN cluster-CA cert at handshake) and the cross-tenant forwarded-credential case is deleted (no forward happens on Kind; TEST-1 item 5's no-credential 401 pins that the listener chain runs the auth middleware, and the tenant check is the same handler mux). The `X-Lenny-Calling-Session` public-listener case stays. Spec line becomes `13.2.4, 29.3` — BECAUSE allowed-vs-dropped is decided by exit 7/0 versus 28 at the CNI and needs no listener, which is the network_policy_test.go / ops_network_policy_test.go convention — ALTERNATIVES: read the applied NetworkPolicy objects (helm-unittest already pins rendering; does not prove the CNI matches selector and port); move the SAN case to TEST-2's subprocess lane (duplicates TEST-1 item 3); move the cross-tenant case to TEST-2 (adds a case for pre-existing handler authorization); turn mtls on in the Kind overlay (breaks adapter dials, see standing WATCHOUT on e2e-values.yaml).
WATCHOUT: the gateway Services select `app.kubernetes.io/name` plus `lenny.dev/component: gateway`, while the NetworkPolicies select `lenny.dev/component: gateway` alone. A gateway-labelled probe that also carries `app.kubernetes.io/name` becomes a lenny-gateway Service endpoint. `networkProbePod` adds only `lenny.dev/test` plus the labels passed. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:1714-1716 (Service selector); tests/tier9_security/network_policy_test.go:294-298
FACT: The Service does not expose 50054 (CHART-1 adds only a container port), so the probe targets a pod IP; `podIPBySelector(t, c, lennySystemNS, "lenny.dev/component=gateway")` exists. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier9_security/ops_network_policy_test.go:517; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:1717-1733
FACT: kindnet drops a denied SYN (curl exit 28); a no-listener allowed target answers RST (exit 7). The existing tier-9 positive controls assert `exitCode != 28`. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier9_security/network_policy_test.go:118-126; tests/tier9_security/ops_network_policy_test.go:192
CORRECTS [review-log OPEN at :303 and :390]: closed by this design; the tier-9 refusal is now discriminating through the allowed/denied probe pair on the same pod IP and port.

### [non-spec.2.review-client-surface.1]
FACT: The proposal changes no served client schema. No OpenAPI route, MCP tool InputSchema, proto, JSONL schema, CRD, or SDK type changes. The only client-visible deltas are new TARGET_NOT_READY triggers, DLQ receipts on not-delivered messages, and the §11.5 no-store rule, and earlier rounds refuted the docs gaps for all three as polish. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json (POST /terminate, DELETE /sessions/{id}, /interrupt, and /resume list only 200, so the OpenAPI 409 gap predates this proposal)
FACT: The coordinator's /mcp handler is stateless: no Mcp-Session-Id and no initialize gate. A bare forwarded tools/call therefore dispatches. Its tool-level idempotency covers only lenny/create_session and lenny/delegate_task, and neither is forwarded. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcp/mcp.go:291-370; cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:710-719
FACT: The holder value never reaches a client surface. sessionserver replicaID is used only for the lease (coordination_lease.go:35-96), and coordinator_replica is in no admin API or OpenAPI schema. EVIDENCE: grep -i coordinator openapi.json returns only the `resuming` enum note
WATCHOUT: The Go client SDK auto-retries any envelope with retryable=true (sdks/client/go/lenny/client.go:443-447). An outcome-unknown 409 TARGET_NOT_READY on POST /messages can therefore be retried with no sender id. §29.3 rule 7 accepts that duplicate on purpose, so it is not a finding.
UNVERIFIED: §11.3 names Helm values gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds and gateway.uploadTimeoutSeconds. CODE-1 adds flags for them, but CHART-1 does not render either value, and neither exists in the chart today, so the gap is pre-existing. The chart/config lens should decide whether it matters. EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:208-209; grep in charts/lenny/templates finds no LENNY_REQUEST_TIMEOUT

### [non-spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The code persists `input_required` and `resuming` as distinct row states. §15.1 terminate and interrupt admit `input_required`, and DELETE admits `resuming`. A `forwardStates` table copied from the spec's state names (`running`, `resume_pending`) misses them, and the helper then returns outcomeLocal on a non-holder. — EVIDENCE: pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:303-342; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go:312; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:335-341 (PodHeld rows, input_required included, hold the lease)
FACT: handleInterrupt with no local binding falls through to a row-only `suspended` write, so an unforwarded off-holder interrupt writes state on the wrong replica. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:66-120
FACT: parseMessageBatch decodes r.Body through a streaming json.Decoder and keeps no bytes, so a forward placed after decode has neither "the bytes it already read" nor an unread body. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:30-35; json_body.go:23-28
FACT: The REST message path waits for the runtime's full turn reply (PodExecutor.Send → runTurn → awaitReply), not for stdin consumption, so a coordinator's message response can take longer than the 30 s --request-timeout bound CODE-1 puts on the forward. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:106-131; podstream.go:256-296
FACT: §11.3 names Helm values gateway.requestTimeoutSeconds and gateway.uploadTimeoutSeconds, but charts/lenny defines neither. Only the bootstrap Job's requestTimeoutSeconds exists. — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:208-209; charts/lenny/values.yaml:4196
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 (S7, depends only on S6) asserts the keyed resume and keyed /mcp cancel_session no-store behaviour, but the resume and cancel_session forward sites are CODE-2 (S8). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:232, checklist S7/S8

### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: The tree's REST message delivery to a running session blocks for the whole agent turn: deliverNow runs executor.Send on d.r.Context(), and PodExecutor.Send waits for the turn's `response` before returning. The on-holder path has no request deadline, so the round-1 forward bound (`--request-timeout`, 30s, CODE-1 item 8 **Client.**) cuts off-holder message forwards whose turn runs longer than 30s, and does so before the coordinator answers. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:233,243; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:95-105
WATCHOUT: Even a spec-conforming coordinator spends up to the §7.2 delivery timeout (30s default) before it falls through to `queued`. That equals the forward bound, so the `queued` fall-through cannot be reached off-holder. The review-log WATCHOUT on the bound covered only terminate seals. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:328; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:208
FACT: The failure modes were traced again this round. Redis reset: lease reads go through leasestore.Failover (session_leases), and the DLQ is Redis-only, so a hold answers inbox_unavailable. Graceful drain: the forward listener closes at SIGTERM, after the preStop handoff. Neither is a finding. USEFUL [review-log line 209-210, 524]: these entries saved the Redis-fallback trace.

### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The §11.5 middleware already releases the pending claim row on a 5xx and on a streamed (flushed) response, so the CODE-1 item 8 `DoNotStore` gate releasing "as it does for a 5xx" matches an existing code path. A helper that calls Flush while copying a forwarded body would also mark the response streamed, which skips caching for every forwarded response. TEST-1 item 4 ("a delivered coordinator 409 without the mark is stored") pins against that. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/idempotency/idempotency.go:443-475, :560-578
FACT: The pre-running watchdog is a store sweep, not a coordinator timer. A `starting` session whose coordinator died, with no adopter (§10.1.1 says "Adoption of a `starting` session is not stated"), therefore still fails within maxStartingTimeoutSeconds, so a terminate/delete in `starting` that gets the no-holder 409 is bounded. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:2-12; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:28
FACT: The coordination lease store is wired only when Redis is configured (Failover over Redis plus leasepg), which matches the CODE-1 item 4 premise. During a Redis outage, Get falls to Postgres, which has no row until the Sweeper re-Acquires there. So off-holder and on-holder forward-gated requests see "no holder" (409, or a DLQ hold) for at most one sweep. That is a bounded fail-closed window, not a defect. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:208-221; pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:27-32
FACT: The elicitation await does poll the store (D13 holds). Tests assert the poll cycle and the exit on deletion. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/elicitation_test.go:525-533, :1090-1095
FACT: A failed pod send on the coordinator answers EXECUTOR_FAILURE through `executorFailure` and buffers nothing. A coordinator that loses the lease after its rule-5 check and is then rejected at the pod therefore does not silently drop a message. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:337-338
WATCHOUT: The Kind e2e overlay runs 2 gateway replicas with global.devMode=true and mtls.enabled=false, so after CODE-2 every off-holder forward there takes not-delivered (no forwarder in dev mode). Any tier-5/8/9 case that expects a forward to succeed, or expects the 50054 listener to reject a bad SAN, cannot run on that overlay. It needs the tier-4 subprocess lane with real certs. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:42-43, :339-340; charts/lenny/values.yaml:2153

This already breaks the NET-060 and Token Service DNS-SAN checks, so 0101 did not introduce it and it is not a finding against 0101. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:322-338; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:337-343
FACT: platformtools.Bridge.CallPlatformTool already resolves the calling session with GetByID(sessionID), with no tenant argument, and installs an agent principal from that row. The X-Lenny-Calling-Session path on the forward listener reuses this existing trust pattern. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/gatewaycontrol/platformtools/platformtools.go:105-123

### [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: TEST-2 item 5's cross-tenant case is a direct request to the coordinator's forward listener in the subprocess lane (gateway client certificate, tenant-A session, valid tenant-B credential) asserting 404 `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` and no write — BECAUSE a foreign credential sent to the serving replica gets a local 404 before the forward point (handleTerminalWrite resolves tenant from the principal; pgstore.Get filters id AND tenant_id), so only a gateway-certificate peer can reach the coordinator's rule 3 check, and a coordinator that trusted the peer would write — ALTERNATIVES: move forwardToHolder before the entry read (fails open across tenants, adds an untenanted second row read CODE-2 rules out); delete the case (leaves rule 3's foreign-credential authorization unpinned; TEST-1 item 5 covers only the absent credential); assert serving-replica 404 with no forward (pre-existing handler behaviour, never exercises rule 3).
WATCHOUT: no cross-tenant case may say "is forwarded". The serving replica refuses a foreign-tenant request before the forward point on REST and on `lenny/send_message` (row read and cross-tenant check precede ForwardToolCall, CODE-2). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go handleTerminalWrite (resolveTenant then store.Get, ErrNotFound → 404); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/request_identity.go lookupTenant; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go Get ("A cross-tenant miss is indistinguishable from a missing row").
MISTAKE: the round-2 restoration of the cross-tenant sentence wrote it as a forwarded off-holder terminate, which the staged placement (CODE-2 item 1) makes impossible; it cost a round-3 finding.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-6 Tier 9 negative probe source is the unrestricted `default` namespace, run after the CoreDNS egress positive control of `TestOpsDenyAllIngress_spec_25_4` — BECAUSE `lenny-agents` and the `app: lenny-ops` source both sit under default-deny egress that admits gateway pods only on grpcPort/$gatewayPort, so their exit 28 held whatever allow-gateway-ingress admitted on 50054; a default-namespace source has no egress policy, so only the gateway ingress policy can drop it — ALTERNATIVES: test-scoped NetworkPolicy granting the old probes egress (no tier9 test creates a NetworkPolicy; mutates shared namespace policy); deleting the "discriminates" clause (leaves the NET-072 50054 ingress restriction with no cluster-tier pin); keeping lenny-agents beside the new probe (pins nothing TEST-6 owns).
WATCHOUT: a negative NetworkPolicy ingress probe is vacuous when its source namespace has default-deny egress; the source must be unrestricted and paired with a positive egress control — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/agent-network-policies.yaml default-deny-all (Ingress, Egress) and gateway egress on gateway.grpcPort only; charts/lenny/templates/ops-network-policies.yaml gateway egress on $gatewayPort only; tests/tier9_security/ops_network_policy_test.go TestOpsDenyAllIngress_spec_25_4 (defaultNS + CoreDNS :9153 control, exit 28 on target).
FACT: allow-gateway-ingress has no portless or all-namespace rule, so a default-namespace source reaching 50054 is admitted only by a mis-rendered 50054 rule — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml allow-gateway-ingress (every `from` carries a `ports` list).

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: TEST-2 item 5's cross-tenant sentence becomes a request sent straight to the coordinator's forward listener in the subprocess lane (gateway client certificate, tenant-A session, valid tenant-B credential), asserting 404 `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` and no write (rule 3). The forward point stays after the entry read. BECAUSE the serving replica's entry read is tenant-scoped (resolveTenant then store.Get filters `id AND tenant_id`), so a foreign credential gets a local 404 and is never forwarded. Rule 3's coordinator-side authentication can only be reached by a peer that holds a gateway certificate, which is exactly the threat rule 3 and NET-072 ("carries no user authority") guard against. ALTERNATIVES: moving forwardToHolder ahead of the entry read (the state gate and holder lookup need the row; an untenanted read would forward, or disclose by timing, sessions of another tenant; fail-open); deleting the sentence (leaves rule 3 authorization unpinned, the round-2 MISTAKE); a tier-1 case in TEST-1 item 5 (would not show "no write" against a real store, and TEST-1 item 5 owns credential carriage only).
FACT: a cross-tenant session request answers 404 at the handler's entry read on any replica, before any forward point. The forward listener runs the same auth middleware and mux, so the coordinator also answers 404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND for a foreign credential. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:51-58; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/request_identity.go:56-58; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:399-406
WATCHOUT: do not write "forwarded" in a cross-tenant test case for any session-addressed route. The `lenny/send_message` handler (non-spec CODE-2, the line "right after its target row read and cross-tenant check") also refuses before ForwardToolCall. Only a direct gateway-cert request to the forward listener reaches the coordinator's rule-3 check. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:96, :120
CORRECTS [non-spec.2 restoration recorded at review-log.md:657]: the restored sentence ("is forwarded, and the coordinator refuses it under rule 3") was not achievable, because the serving replica's tenant-scoped read answers 404 first. The rule-3 case is now pinned by a direct forward-listener request.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-6 Tier 9 negative 50054 probe is sourced from the unrestricted `default` namespace (no NetworkPolicy), behind the CoreDNS egress positive control of `TestOpsDenyAllIngress_spec_25_4`; the `lenny-agents` and `app: lenny-ops` probes are removed — BECAUSE both of those sources run their own default-deny egress (agent-network-policies.yaml default-deny-all, ops-network-policies.yaml gateway egress only on the gateway port), so their exit 28 holds whatever allow-gateway-ingress admits; a default-namespace source can only be dropped by the gateway's ingress policy, and with no forward listener on Kind an admitted connection is refused (exit 7), so exit 28 discriminates — ALTERNATIVES: test-scoped NetworkPolicy granting the probe egress (no tier9 test creates a NetworkPolicy; mutates a shared namespace's policy set; the tree already has the default-namespace ingress pattern); deleting "that timeout is the assertion that discriminates" (leaves the NET-072 ingress restriction, a security predicate, with no cluster-tier pin; helm-unittest checks render only).
FACT: The tree's pattern for probing an INGRESS rule is a source in the unrestricted `default` namespace plus a positive control to CoreDNS 9153; the network_policy_test.go pattern probes EGRESS blocks from lenny-system. Using the egress pattern for an ingress assertion is the trap round 2 fell into — EVIDENCE: tests/tier9_security/ops_network_policy_test.go:265-325, tests/tier9_security/network_policy_test.go:108-136
WATCHOUT: Any probe whose namespace carries a default-deny egress policy (lenny-system unlabelled, lenny-agents, lenny-ops-labelled) times out on 50054 because of its own egress, so it cannot pin allow-gateway-ingress — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/agent-network-policies.yaml:34-43, charts/lenny/templates/ops-network-policies.yaml:60-81
MISTAKE: Round 2 replaced the vacuous no-listener refusal sentences with lenny-agents and lenny-ops probes, which are vacuous for the same reason one layer earlier (source egress rather than listener absence); cost one round.

This matches the existing network_policy_test.go convention, so it was not filed. — EVIDENCE: system-network-policies.yaml:843-905, agent-network-policies.yaml:69-99

### [non-spec.3.respec-forward-deadline.1]
DECISION: applied [non-spec.2.fix-design-G6.1] whole: CODE-1 item 1 carries no timeout flags, CODE-1 item 8 **Client.** is the home of the forward's dial bound and of its absent request deadline, TEST-1 item 4 pins both, and summary **Request deadline unenforced.** records the gateway-wide gap — BECAUSE as [non-spec.2.fix-design-G6.1] — ALTERNATIVES: as [non-spec.2.fix-design-G6.1]; deleting the superseded WATCHOUT lines in place (the ledger retires entries through CORRECTS, and in-place deletion leaves the references in [non-spec.2.fix-G6.1] and [non-spec.2.fix-design-G6.1] pointing at nothing).

CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 and non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: their forward-bound DECISIONs and their terminate-seal WATCHOUTs ("terminate and delete forwards are bounded by the 30s request timeout", "with the 30s bound, an off-holder terminate ...") are superseded. CORRECTS [non-spec.2.review-client-surface.1 UNVERIFIED, non-spec.2.review-edit-sites.1 FACT, and the flag and per-kind FACTs of non-spec.2.review-mechanism.1, non-spec.2.review-performance.1, non-spec.3.review-fresh.1, non-spec.3.review-mechanism.1, non-spec.3.review-performance.1, non-spec.3.review-single-source.1, and non-spec.3.review-test-coverage.1]: they describe the `--request-timeout`/`--upload-timeout` flags or the round-3 per-kind bound, neither of which exists after this entry; the UNVERIFIED is discharged.
FACT: every forwarded handler on the holder runs on r.Context() with no gateway deadline; interrupt passes its 5s deadline to the adapter as a parameter — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:976-980; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:229-234; interrupt.go:197-203; upload_to_session.go:70-71; resume.go:96-97; session_transitions.go:51-54; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:95-106.
FACT: with DialTLSContext set, net/http ignores TLSClientConfig and TLSHandshakeTimeout — EVIDENCE: `go doc net/http.Transport`, DialTLSContext field.
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a forward-only request bound or a per-kind table; the gap is gateway-wide and is recorded in the summary. The 2-second context spans both the dial and the handshake inside DialTLSContext — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Client.**
WATCHOUT: replaced mechanism: the round-3 per-kind forward bound (`--upload-timeout` for upload-to-session, `--request-timeout` for the other non-message kinds, message kinds unbounded) and its two flags.
WATCHOUT: it lost because a request served on the coordinating replica runs on its request context with no deadline, so a forward-only bound answers 409 for an effect the coordinator completed, and the message-kind exemption existed only to patch that case.
WATCHOUT: the request-deadline gap and the `PodExecutor.Send` path-2 departure are gateway-wide tree defects; they belong in summary **Request deadline unenforced.** and BUILD-GAPS, not in the forward helper.
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 4's 35-second row is the case that fails if a forward-only 30-second bound returns; shortening it removes that guard (see the OPEN below).
OPEN: OD1, the 35-second tier-1 wait in TEST-1 item 4's no-deadline row. Option A keeps 35 seconds (the only discriminating case against a reintroduced 30-second bound; one parallel wait inside the unit-tier timeout). Option B shortens it to about 3 seconds (pins only that the 2-second dial context does not cover the request). Default Option A is applied in the text, per the owner's standing preference for discriminating tests on failure paths.

DECISION: REG-COORDLEASE Store-cell gap closed by extending SPEC-1 (e) with a second cell replacement in the same row (Store `Redis` -> `Redis, and Postgres during a Redis outage ([§10.1.1](10_gateway-internals.md#1011-stateless-replicas-and-per-session-coordination) **Fallback:**)`), Key cell unchanged; summary deliverable-index SPEC-1 entry names "Store and Semantics cells" — BECAUSE §28.2 makes Store a register column (spec/28_communication-channels.md:75) and SPEC-2 (a) names the Postgres row as a REG-COORDLEASE read source, so the register is the one home for where the register lives — ALTERNATIVES: dropping "`REG-COORDLEASE`" from SPEC-2 (a) rejected, because rule 1 and rule 6 key on the REG-COORDLEASE holder read and would then not cover the fallback, which would need a rule-1 clause; editing the Key cell to name a table rejected, because SPEC-2 (a) names no table and the Fallback pointer locates the row.

FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` lock on the session row (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:29, spec/28_communication-channels.md:252-255), "Postgres advisory locks" (spec/12 LeaseStore row), and after SPEC-2 (a) an unnamed "Postgres lease row keyed by tenant and session" that can be confused with REG-COORDMIRROR's sweeper-written `coordination_lease` table (spec/28:140). This is a candidate separate finding (the fallback's holder row has no table name), and this round leaves it unfixed.

### [spec.10.review-performance.1]
- DECISION: no findings on the staged spec edits under the performance/failure-mode lens — BECAUSE forwarding adds one holder read per off-holder request plus at most a 2 s dial/handshake per forward; CH-BINDRELEASE publishes once per full revoke or §12.8 drain on one platform-scoped pub/sub channel (low rate, no hot-key write); the primary read in SPEC-6 (c) runs only on the replica holding the binding; SPEC-2 (a) Postgres lease row is the shipped session_leases path (D19), so no new etcd or Postgres write load — ALTERNATIVES: filing coordinator-crash dial stalls (each off-holder request to a crashed coordinator's sessions waits up to 2 s until the 60 s lease lapses) rejected, because the shipped §29.3 rows already forward and fail closed on an unreachable coordinator, and the 2 s bound caps the stall.
- DECISION: the compose advertise-address OPEN ([spec.7.review-client-surface.1]) is not a spec-loop finding — BECAUSE §17.4 Compose Mode runs one gateway ("Starts: gateway, controller-sim, single agent pod, ..." spec/17_deployment-topology.md:228), so the holder is always the gateway itself and the address is never dialled, and the remedy (advertise the container IP rather than the service name) is in non-spec CODE-1 item 5 (non-spec-changes.md:53) — ALTERNATIVES: widening SPEC-2 (b) `<pod IP>` to cover compose (adds a spec mode for a value nothing dials).
- WATCHOUT: a delivered CH-BINDRELEASE request whose SPEC-6 (c) primary read fails during a Postgres failover also leaves the binding held; the card names only a dropped request. Same as shipped code, so not filed — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:178-179, :339

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-1 item 8 **State gate** now returns `outcomeLocal` when the state is outside `forwardStates[kind]`, or the state is `suspended` and `holdsPod(row)` is false — BECAUSE a per-kind set of state values cannot express "suspended while the row names a pod", and the gate is the one place REST terminate/delete, MCP `lenny/cancel_session`, and resume all read — ALTERNATIVES: drop `suspended` from the terminate set (reopens F-10.1.33 off-holder terminal write); synthetic `suspended_with_pod` key (contradicts CODE-2 item 2's literal-row-value trap); `coordination.podlessSuspended` (unexported, other package); pod check at each call site (rule in several homes).
FACT: `holdsPod` is `row.PodAssignment != "" || s.podBinder == nil`; with no pod binder it returns true, but `coordinatesSession`'s guard also returns true then, so the gate's **Lease read** step makes the outcome local with no read — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go:167-169, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:89
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 4's discriminating case for the gate is the podless `suspended` terminate (suspended is inside the terminate set); a test using only a state absent from every set cannot catch a gate with no pod predicate — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 4 last bullet

### [non-spec.4.fix-G2.1]
- DECISION: `messagingLimiter.allow` splits by key into a sender-keyed method (`msg:out` window and the in-process lifetime cap) run on the serving replica before `ForwardToolCall` and skipped when `sessionserver.Forwarded(ctx)` is true, and a target-keyed method (`msg:in`) run among the post-forward steps — BECAUSE §8.3 `maxPerSession` is a per-session lifetime cap and the lifetime map is process-local, so counting it on each target's coordinator gave a sender one cap per coordinating replica; with the sender part ahead of the forward the not-delivered rerun of "those steps" counts nothing twice — ALTERNATIVES: Redis lifetime key (needs a §12.4 row, fixes the pre-existing restart-reset gap outside 0101); moving only the lifetime map (rests on an implementation detail rather than the key); running scope on the serving replica too (second sender read, loses TEST-2 item 9's SCOPE_DENIED-from-coordinator discriminator); accepting the split (relaxes a §8.3/§13.5 security bound); amending staged rule 4 (rule 4 stays true).
- FACT: `msg:out` and `msg:in` already run on the Redis fixed-window counter shared by all replicas; only `lifetime` (messagingLimiter.lifetime, `allowLifetime`) is in-process — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/messaging.go:58-117; caller pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:322 (sole caller of `allow`).
- WATCHOUT: today's `allow` increments the lifetime counter last so a per-minute rejection consumes no lifetime budget. After the split a target-side `msg:in` rejection runs after the sender-keyed method, so it does consume a lifetime slot. Not staged; an implementor who wants the old property must order it inside the sender-keyed method only (msg:out before lifetime) — EVIDENCE: messaging.go:84-95 doc comment.
- WATCHOUT: `senderID` (`callerSessionID`) and the `msg_` id assignment currently sit after the cross-tenant check, inside the scope block; the sender-keyed limit needs senderID before the forward, and its RATE_LIMITED receipt needs a message id. The forwarded `tools/call` carries the original arguments, so the coordinator assigns its own id when the sender gave none — EVIDENCE: mcptools_register.go:292-317.
- WATCHOUT: staged spec rule 6 says the replica applying rule 6 "runs the authorization, scope, and rate-limit checks of the route or tool". On a rule-5 receiver the sender-keyed part is skipped (counted at the origin), so that receiver runs only `msg:in`. The design ruled rule 6 not a site (the tool's limits do all run once per message across the path); do not reopen the converged spec for it — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md rule 6, around "authorization, scope, and rate-limit checks".
- CORRECTS [Standing context Settled **messagingRateLimit.**]: rule 4 does not exempt the coordinator from the §8.3 limit, but the coordinator now applies only the target-keyed `msg:in` part to a forwarded send; the sender-keyed part (`msg:out`, lifetime) runs on the serving replica (CODE-2 item 6).
- CORRECTS [Standing context Traps **send_message forward placement.**]: the forward now follows the sender-keyed limits; it still precedes scope, the target-keyed `msg:in` limit, inReplyTo, and PreMessageDelivery, and must not move after those.
- CORRECTS [Standing context Deferred **Sender rate-limit split.**], [reconcile.2 / spec.4.review-security.1 OPEN on the sender rate-limit split]: closed by CODE-2 item 6 in round non-spec.4; delete the Deferred entry at compaction.
- FACT: TEST-2 item 9 now carries the in-scope not-delivered hold (`queued`, MCP client and platform call), the post-connection-failure `TARGET_NOT_READY` no-hold for `lenny/send_message`, and the subprocess-lane cross-replica `maxPerSession` 3 case whose "fourth send and not before" assertion catches both the per-coordinator split and a double count on the held send.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G3.1]
FACT: tests/testinfra/gateway/gateway.go draws one port (`freePort`), passes it as the plain-HTTP REST `--addr`, sets defaults through `cmd.Env` (LENNY_TLS_TERMINATED_UPSTREAM, LENNY_DEV_MODE, LENNY_REDIS_ALLOW_INSECURE) so a test's extra flags win, and exposes only `BaseURL()` on `Process`. Advertising "the harness port" would point every forward at the REST listener, and two subprocesses on the default `:50054` listen address collide — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/gateway/gateway.go:86-90, :101, :132-137, :194
DECISION: CODE-1 item 5 harness bullet now draws a second free port per process, sets both LENNY_INTER_REPLICA_LISTEN_ADDR and LENNY_INTER_REPLICA_ADVERTISE_ADDR to 127.0.0.1:<that port>, and exposes the address on `Process` beside `BaseURL` — BECAUSE it fixes the bind collision and the wrong advertised port in one bullet and gives TEST-2 item 5 the coordinator's forward-listener address — ALTERNATIVES: harness flags in baseArgs (breaks the env-default convention); changing the item 1 `:50054` default (the chart sets it explicitly, and `:0` cannot be advertised); describing the wiring in TEST-2 (second statement of the harness rule).
WATCHOUT: the compose advertise address (service name rather than a pod IP) in CODE-1 item 5 remains a separate deferred item; this fix did not touch it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 5 compose bullet

### [non-spec.4.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: TEST-3 invariant is "no send delivered twice, none both delivered and held, every 2xx receipt is a delivery or a DLQ hold; a 409 or 500 refusal is accepted with or without delivery (§29.3 Forwarding. rules 5 and 7)" — BECAUSE rule 7 can answer 409 after a delivery and a rule-5 handoff loss at the pod answers 500 EXECUTOR_FAILURE (sessionserver/messages_delivery.go `executorFailure`) — ALTERNATIVES: listing each rule's outcome in TEST-3 (restates SPEC-5 rules); exempting fault-injected cases (rule-5 loss arises without fault injection).
WATCHOUT: a checker that reads a 409 or 500 refusal as "not delivered" fails conforming code; the refusal places no constraint on delivery. The "both delivered and held" assertion is the one that discriminates the reused-connection classification trap. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 (a) rules 5 and 7.
WATCHOUT: the TEST-5 Redis-outage case must wait until a replica holds the lease in the Postgres fallback before sending. A Redis-held lease does not migrate: pgstore.Renew returns ErrNotHeld when no `session_leases` row matches, so the coordinator drops coordination and the Sweeper re-adopts through the fallback. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go Renew; cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:210 `leasestore.NewFailover(leaseStore, leasepg.New(pgPool), nil)`.
DECISION: TEST-1 item 4 dialler deny case names a server with a cluster-CA certificate carrying a different DNS SAN or an agent SPIFFE certificate — BECAUSE only a cluster-CA-signed wrong-SAN certificate fails a client that omits ServerName or sets InsecureSkipVerify; an unrelated-CA certificate is rejected by RootCAs alone — ALTERNATIVES: placing it in item 3 (accepting side only).
DECISION: TEST-1 item 4 adds "A lease read error" to the not-delivered list without "and none dials" — BECAUSE none of those branches can dial; not-delivered versus local is the discriminating assertion.
DECISION: summary deliverables index TEST-5 line cut to "chaos cases" so it enumerates nothing and stays true as cases are added.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-1 item 8 **State gate** names the pod predicate in code: the helper returns `outcomeLocal` when the state is not in `forwardStates[kind]`, or is `suspended` and `holdsPod(row)` is false. CODE-2 item 2 stays the home of the state list and its "while the row names a pod" / "with a pod" qualifiers, unchanged. TEST-1 item 4's last bullet gains the podless-suspended terminate case — BECAUSE a per-kind set of state values cannot express a pod qualifier, and `holdsPod` is the predicate resume.go already uses to choose `resumeHeldPodRequest` — ALTERNATIVES: drop `suspended` from forwardStates (reopens F-10.1.33 for a pod-holding suspended terminate); a synthetic `suspended_with_pod` key in the table (invents a row value the trap says is matched literally); use coordination.podlessSuspended (unexported in another package, and it differs from holdsPod only with podBinder nil, where coordinatesSession's guard returns true so the gate's outcome is local anyway).
FACT: `holdsPod(row)` is `row.PodAssignment != "" || s.podBinder == nil`; `coordinatesSession` returns true when leaseStore, podBinder, or replicaID is unset, so a no-binder server never reaches a lease read — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go:167-169, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:88-91
FACT: a podless suspended row holds no coordination lease — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:344-350; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:580 terminate row.
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 4's "a state outside the gate runs locally" cannot catch this defect, because `suspended` is inside forwardStates[terminate]. The podless-suspended terminate row is the discriminating unit case.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: The `lenny/send_message` handler applies the sender-keyed messaging limits (`msg:out` and the lifetime cap) on the serving replica before `ForwardToolCall`, skipped when `sessionserver.Forwarded(ctx)` is true; the target-keyed `msg:in` limit stays where `allow` sits today, so the coordinator (or the serving replica on not-delivered) runs it. `messagingLimiter.allow` splits into a sender-keyed and a target-keyed method. — BECAUSE the lifetime counter is process-local, so counting it on each target's coordinator gives a sender one cap per coordinating replica; with the sender part at the handler top, the not-delivered path reruns only the remaining steps and nothing counts twice. — ALTERNATIVES: a Redis-backed lifetime counter (fixes the split and the pre-existing restart reset, but adds a new Redis key that §12.4 must register, i.e. a spec edit in a non-spec round, and it fixes a pre-existing gap); moving only the lifetime counter (rationale by implementation detail rather than by key); running the scope check on the serving replica too (removes TEST-2 item 9's coordinator SCOPE_DENIED discriminator); accepting the split as best-effort (relaxes a §13.5 security bound).
FACT: `msg:out` and `msg:in` run on a Redis fixed-window counter shared by every replica (`w.rateLimiter = ratelimitredis.New(...)`, cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:288,329, wired as `MessagingRateCounter` at cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go:356). Only the lifetime map is in-process (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/messaging.go:65-69, `allowLifetime`). — EVIDENCE: as cited.
FACT: Today's handler order is row read, TARGET_TERMINAL, cross-tenant, scope, msg id, `allow` (out, in, lifetime), inReplyTo, PreMessageDelivery, `sm.deliver` (mcptools_register.go:272-362). Edge `/mcp` senders were already load-balanced across replicas before 0101, so their lifetime count was already split; the regression is for agent platform calls, which were counted on the replica holding the sender's LNK-GWCONTROL.
WATCHOUT: Under the new order an out-of-scope send and a send refused by `msg:in` consume sender budget, and RATE_LIMITED wins over SCOPE_DENIED for a sender over its limit. No spec text orders scope against rate limiting (§7.2 orders only cross-tenant first). Do not "fix" this by running scope on the serving replica. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:280, mcptools_register.go:283-285.
WATCHOUT: Staged rule 4 lists only §11.1, §11.5, and PreToolResult as what the coordinator skips; the sender-keyed messaging skip lives in CODE-2 item 6 and rule 4 stays true (it does not claim the coordinator applies everything else, and §8.3 names no replica). Rule 4's §11.1 skip is also the precedent for a serving-replica counter write before the forward, so rule 1's "before it writes any state" is not read as covering rate-limit counters. A reviewer filing "rule 4 omits the messaging skip" should be pointed here.
CORRECTS [Standing context Traps: send_message forward placement]: the forward now comes after the sender-keyed messaging limit and before scope, the target-keyed limit, inReplyTo, and PreMessageDelivery. "Do not move it after those steps" still holds for those four.
CORRECTS [Standing context Settled: messagingRateLimit]: rule 4 still does not exempt the coordinator, but CODE-2 item 6 has the coordinator skip the sender-keyed part on a forwarded call; it applies only `msg:in`.
CORRECTS [Deferred: Sender rate-limit split], [spec.8 ledger OPEN: Sender rate-limit split], [reconcile.2 OPEN: sender rate-limit split]: closed by CODE-2 item 6 as redesigned in non-spec round 4; delete at compaction.
DECISION: Finding 5 closes by extending TEST-2 item 9 in place (in-scope hold returning `queued` from an MCP client and as a platform call; post-connection failure returning TARGET_NOT_READY with an empty DLQ), plus one subprocess-lane rate-limit case whose "fourth send and not before" assertion catches both the cross-replica split and a double count on the held path.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: CODE-1 item 5's harness bullet is replaced in place: the harness draws a second free port per process, sets both LENNY_INTER_REPLICA_LISTEN_ADDR and LENNY_INTER_REPLICA_ADVERTISE_ADDR to 127.0.0.1:<that port>, and exposes the address on Process (accessor beside BaseURL) for TEST-2 item 5's direct forward-listener request — BECAUSE the harness's only port is the plain-HTTP REST --addr, so advertising it makes every TLS forward fail at handshake, and leaving the listen default :50054 makes the second subprocess fail to bind — ALTERNATIVES: harness passes --inter-replica-* flags in baseArgs (works, but env keeps the existing "harness defaults env, test flags win" convention); changing the item 1 default (the chart sets the listen address explicitly, so the default is not the defect); editing TEST-2 lead/item 5 (both stay true once the harness exposes the address).
FACT: tests/testinfra/gateway/gateway.go draws exactly one port (freePort, :86-90), passes it as --addr (:101), sets env only for TLS_TERMINATED_UPSTREAM, DEV_MODE, REDIS_ALLOW_INSECURE (:132-137), and exposes only BaseURL (:194). A test reaching the forward listener needs a new accessor. EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/gateway/gateway.go:86-145, :194
WATCHOUT: the compose advertise-address OPEN (service name versus SPEC-2 (b) pod IP) is the other bullet of the same item 5 and is not touched by this fix; the harness 127.0.0.1 value is the same non-Kubernetes class of departure. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 5 compose bullet

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: TEST-3 invariant is replaced by three rule-derived assertions (no message both delivered and held; the forward path delivers no message twice; every 2xx receipt is a delivery or a DLQ hold), with a rule 7 409 and the rule 5 pod rejection accepted with or without delivery — BECAUSE rule 6 holds messages (old text omitted holds) and rules 5/7 produce non-2xx answers whose delivery is unknown or absent — ALTERNATIVES: naming 500 EXECUTOR_FAILURE in staged text (rejected: no spec section states the client answer to a rule-5 pod rejection, so the test would pin unspecified code).
WATCHOUT: today the rule-5 late-handoff rejection surfaces as 500 EXECUTOR_FAILURE from restDelivery.executorFailure — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:338-347 (rerouteUnbound :320-334 picks it when the fresh row is running).
DECISION: lease-read error joins TEST-1 item 4's not-delivered bullet; the dialler-side NET-072 deny case replaces "a handshake failure" in the dial bullet (server with a cluster-CA cert carrying another DNS SAN, or an agent SPIFFE cert) — BECAUSE "not delivered" already discriminates InsecureSkipVerify / missing ServerName (those would deliver) — ALTERNATIVES: a new item-3 dialler case (rejected: item 3 is the listener file; the classification bullet is where a dial failure is asserted).
FACT: with DialTLSContext the handshake runs inside the dial, so httptrace GotConn fires after the handshake and a handshake failure classifies as not delivered — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Client.** and **Classification**.
FACT: Failover.Get routes to the Postgres fallback on a Redis infrastructure error, and Redis leases are not copied to Postgres; the Sweeper's next pass (default --coordination-interval 15s) re-acquires through the fallback — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:27-31,99-108; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:548.
WATCHOUT: the TEST-5 Redis-outage case must send after the coordinator's next sweep (before it, Get returns no holder, rule 6 enqueues to the Redis DLQ and the answer is inbox_unavailable), and inside the §11.1 rate-limit fail-open window (rateLimitFailOpenMaxSeconds, 60s, then 429) or with that cap raised — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:218,234; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:652-657.
UNVERIFIED: whether coordinator-side delivery to a running pod writes anything to Redis (event relay) that fails the receipt during an outage; the TEST-5 implementor checks.
OPEN: TEST-1 item 2 says "a pgstore round-trip" but two pgstore packages exist (pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/pgstore, the fallback lease; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/pgstore, the mirror); the item does not name which. Separate finding for a later round.
### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: TEST-3 invariant is replaced in place with "no send delivered twice, none both delivered and held, every 2xx receipt is a delivery or a DLQ hold", plus one clause that a 409 or 500 refusal is accepted with or without delivery (cites §29.3 **Forwarding.** rules 5 and 7) — BECAUSE rule 7 (unknown outcome, 409, possibly delivered) and a rule-5 handoff loss (pod rejects on coordination_generation; delivery path answers 500 EXECUTOR_FAILURE, sessionserver/messages_delivery.go executorFailure) are both conforming outcomes that "delivered exactly once or refused with 409" fails — ALTERNATIVES: enumerating each accepted outcome per rule (restates the rules in the test list).
DECISION: the lease-read-error case goes into TEST-1 item 4's existing not-delivered bullet as a list entry; the Redis-outage forward is one new TEST-5 bullet; the summary TEST-5 index line becomes "chaos cases" so it stops listing TEST-5's contents.
FACT: a coordinator whose Redis lease is in force when Redis stops does NOT carry it into Postgres: Failover.Renew falls back to pgstore.Renew, which only UPDATEs an existing session_leases row and returns ErrNotHeld when none exists. Coordination moves only after a replica re-acquires through the Postgres fallback (Sweeper adoption). A Redis-outage forward test must wait for that re-acquisition. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:80-87, pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:118-138
FACT: the sessionserver coordination lease store is the Failover wrapper whenever Postgres is wired (w.coordLeaseStore = leaseStore after NewFailover), and Failover.Get routing to the fallback is already unit-tested (TestFailover_InfraErrorRoutesToFallback_spec_12_4). The composite-holder pg round trip is TEST-1 item 2. The new chaos case pins the end-to-end wiring only. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:207-221, pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover_test.go:101
WATCHOUT: the dialler-side NET-072 deny case has to use a server that presents a cluster-CA-signed certificate (with a non-gateway DNS SAN, or an agent SPIFFE certificate). A server with a certificate from an unrelated CA also fails under a client with RootCAs but no ServerName, so it cannot discriminate a missing ServerName. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Client.**

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
FACT: The coordination lease store is `leasestore.NewFailover(redis, leasepg)` whenever Postgres is wired, and `Failover.Get` falls back to Postgres only on an infrastructure error; a healthy-but-empty Redis (reset) returns ErrNotFound without consulting Postgres. So CODE-1 item 8's single lease read survives a Redis outage (D19 holds), and after a Redis reset an off-holder request takes rule 6 until the Sweeper re-acquires. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:208-221, pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:27-31,103-109
FACT: The shipped §11.4 subscriber already reads the Postgres primary per held binding (ReleaseRevokedBinding → readPrimary), so SPEC-6 (c) and the CODE-3 drain publish add no new Postgres dependency or failover regression; each pub/sub channel has its own Subscribe goroutine, so a long tenant-wide release loop on `lenny:session:terminate` does not stall the token or deny-list fan-outs. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:81-92, pkg/gateway/storage/pubsub/bus.go:74-92, cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:40 (20 s per release)
FACT: Shipped shutdown runs httpSrv.Shutdown then sessionSrv.Close and leaves leases to lapse (60 s); draining the forward listener alongside the REST listener (CODE-1 item 6) is no worse than shipped, because the process stops coordinating at that point anyway. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:213-229
FACT: `lenny_idempotency_cache_skipped_total` appears in no spec/ or docs/ page, so the new `forward_refused` reason (CODE-1 item 8 **Idempotency gate**) needs only the Go doc-comment edits CODE-1 already targets. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics.go:913-918; grep of spec/ docs/ returns nothing
DECISION: No finding on the compose advertise address OPEN — BECAUSE compose runs one gateway in dev mode with no adapter cert flags, so the holder is always self and no forwarder is built; agrees with spec.10/11/12 decisions — ALTERNATIVES: filing a SPEC-2 (b) format mismatch (inert value, no broken behaviour).
DECISION: No finding on the forward client's default idle pool (HTTP/1.1, MaxIdleConnsPerHost 2) — BECAUSE it costs extra TLS handshakes rather than producing wrong behaviour, as [non-spec.1.review-performance.1] judged; USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-performance.1].
DECISION: TEST-1 item 4's 35-second case does not contradict §11.3's 30 s Request timeout — BECAUSE it is a helper unit test under a caller context without a deadline and asserts only that the helper adds none; the gateway-wide deadline gap is the recorded unstaged defect.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the TEST-3 invariant ("delivered exactly once or refused with 409, and never both delivered and held") rejects two outcomes the staged rules produce under the faults TEST-3 injects. A post-connection failure after the coordinator delivered gives delivered plus 409 (rule 7). A forward that reaches a coordinator which loses the lease during handoff is rejected at the pod and answers 500 EXECUTOR_FAILURE (rule 5 second sentence; messages_delivery.go executorFailure) — BECAUSE a checker written as stated fails a conforming implementation — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it to the implementor (the invariant is what the test asserts, so it cannot be delegated).
DECISION: compose advertise-address OPEN not filed — BECAUSE docker-compose.yml runs one `gateway:` service with --dev-mode and no adapter cert flags, so CODE-1 item 4 builds no forwarder and the holder is always self. `gateway:50054` parses under holderAddress and is never dialled. Agrees with [spec.10.review-reliability.1] and [spec.12.review-reliability.1] — EVIDENCE: docker-compose.yml:112-150; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:234
FACT: the CODE-3 publish path is bounded even under context.WithoutCancel. TerminateUserSessions runs terminateLocal, where each release is WithTimeout(userTerminateRPCTimeout), and then pubsub.Bus.Publish, which is a single go-redis PUBLISH under the client's own timeouts. The subscriber applies inline on the loop goroutine — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:80-143; pkg/gateway/storage/pubsub/bus.go:59-64
FACT: lease holder columns are TEXT (session_leases.holder, coordination_lease.coordinator_replica), so the longer composite holder value fits the Postgres fallback — EVIDENCE: migrations/0111_session_leases.up.sql:25; migrations/0164_coordination_lease.up.sql:39
FACT: the only lease-holder comparisons in pkg are the Sweeper (coordination.go priorHolder/Acquire/Release/CoordinatorReplica), sessionserver coordination_lease.go, and barrier wiring ListHeldByReplica. CODE-1 item 3's consumer set covers all of them. sessioncallback GatewayID and the propagator Origin take w.replica but compare no lease — EVIDENCE: grep "s.replicaID|.ReplicaID" pkg/gateway
FACT: graceful shutdown calls httpSrv.Shutdown only after SIGTERM (runserver.go:213-218), so a forward listener drained "alongside" closes at the same point. Peers then take not-delivered until the 60 s lease lapses, which is the bounded crash-takeover window — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:211-228
USEFUL [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]: its executorFailure FACT provided the handoff outcome used in this round's finding.

### [non-spec.7.fix-G1.1]
FACT: The last-colon split in CODE-1 item 2 `coordinationHolder` leaves brackets on the host of `[fd00::1]:50054`; net.JoinHostPort then yields `[[fd00::1]]:50054`, which net.SplitHostPort rejects ("missing port in address"), so holderAddress returns false and every forward to that replica takes rule 6 not-delivered while startup passes. EVIDENCE: go run check in this round; non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 2 first bullet.
DECISION: CODE-1 item 2 now strips one enclosing bracket pair from the host and requires a non-empty host containing no bracket; TEST-1 item 1 states "already-bracketed IPv6, which is returned unchanged" and adds "an unmatched bracket" to the rejected cases — BECAUSE one parse path covers both the chart's unbracketed `$(LENNY_POD_IP):<port>` form and the bracketed form SPEC-2 (b) names, and the no-bracket clause makes `[fd00::1:50054` and `fd00::1]:50054` fail at startup instead of yielding a holder value holderAddress rejects — ALTERNATIVES: SplitHostPort-first with last-colon fallback (two parse paths); self-check with holderAddress before return (extra step); SplitHostPort alone (rejects unbracketed IPv6 "too many colons").
WATCHOUT: Strip-only without the no-bracket host clause reopens the silent misconfiguration for an unmatched bracket (holderAddress: "unexpected '[' in address"). Keep both clauses. Verified outputs: `[fd00::1]:50054`, `fd00::1:50054` -> `[fd00::1]:50054`; `10.0.0.1:50054`, `gw.svc:50054` unchanged; `[fd00::1:50054`, `fd00::1]:50054`, `[]:50054`, `:50054` rejected. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 2, TEST-1 item 1.

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: coordinationHolder strips one enclosing bracket pair from the last-colon host, then requires a non-empty host containing no bracket, then rebuilds with net.JoinHostPort — BECAUSE the last-colon split of "[fd00::1]:50054" yields host "[fd00::1]" and JoinHostPort re-wraps it to "[[fd00::1]]:50054", which net.SplitHostPort (and so holderAddress) rejects; the no-bracket clause makes an unmatched "[fd00::1:50054" fail at startup instead of passing and producing a holder holderAddress rejects ("unexpected '[' in address") — ALTERNATIVES: net.SplitHostPort first with last-colon fallback (two paths for one rule); validating the rebuilt value with holderAddress at startup (adds a step where the parse fix suffices).
WATCHOUT: plain net.SplitHostPort cannot replace the last-colon split, because it rejects the chart's unbracketed "fd00::1:50054" ("too many colons"). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 2 (Holder value), "unbracketed `$(LENNY_POD_IP):<port>` interpolation".

DECISION: export ONE kind type and ONE outcome type shared by `forwardToHolder` and `ForwardToolCall` (`ForwardKind`, `ForwardKindMessageNoReplyTo`, `ForwardOutcome`, `ForwardLocal`, `ForwardDelivered`, `ForwardNotDelivered`, `ForwardUnknown`), renamed at every site in non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 and CODE-2 items 1, 4, and 6 — BECAUSE mcptools (package boundary) must name the kind constants and compare outcomes, and the existing cross-package precedent is the exported `sessionserver.HoldOutcome`/`HoldQueued` — ALTERNATIVES: a separate exported type set for the MCP variant only (two type sets for one core, hair); returning a bool/error triple from ForwardToolCall (loses the not-delivered/unknown split that rule 6 vs rule 7 needs).

If ForwardToolCall is added to that interface the fake must grow the method. The proposal does not name the seam; left to the implementor. EVIDENCE: mcptools.go:709-732.

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
FACT: leasestore.Failover.Get falls back to Postgres only on an infrastructure error. A primary ErrNotFound, which a Redis reset or a lost async-replicated key produces, is returned as "no holder" without consulting Postgres. Under CODE-1 item 8 that is outcomeNotDelivered on every replica, the holder included, until the coordination Sweeper re-acquires the lease (default interval 15s). This is the settled "absent holder is transient" case, so I did not file it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:100-110; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:243.
FACT: the off-holder lease read adds one Redis GET per forwarded request. The shipped coordinatesSession already does a GET per call. The spec's coordination budget is ~10K ops/s at Tier 3 single-tenant, and the spec states no forward-rate budget — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:88-96; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1207.
FACT: TerminateUserSessions runs terminateLocal synchronously before it publishes. On the drain replica that call iterates the registry snapshot and finds nothing to release, because the forced transition's terminal pipeline has already released this replica's bindings — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:80-91,114-134.
WATCHOUT: the REST http.Server sets only ReadHeaderTimeout 10s, and CODE-1 item 6's forward-listener http.Server sets no timeout, so the server-side TLS handshake has no deadline. Only NET-072-validated gateway peers can reach the listener, and their client-side dial is bounded at 2s, so I judged this to be hardening rather than a finding — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:976-980.

### [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]

FACT: Go http.Transport retries a request by itself on a reused connection when nothing was written and the body is rewindable (GetBody set, as for a bytes body). The retry dials a fresh connection after GotConn already fired, so CODE-1 item 8 classifies a failed retry as unknown (409, no hold). That errs toward fail-closed, never toward a double hold. No POST or DELETE forward is replayable in any other way, because the outbound request carries no Idempotency-Key header. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:73 (header allow-list), :75 (GotConn classification)
FACT: holdMessage can return HoldTerminal (TARGET_TERMINAL) as well as queued, dropped, and error. CODE-2 item 4's list of receipts names only those three, but the existing holdOutcomeFor already maps HoldTerminal, and TEST-2 item 2 covers the terminal race, so the gap was judged polish and not filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/held_delivery.go:220-260, messages_delivery.go:509-527
FACT: The REST http.Server sets only ReadHeaderTimeout (10s) and no WriteTimeout, so TEST-1 item 4's 35-second coordinator answer agrees with the serving listener. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:976-980
FACT: The drain publish runs on context.WithoutCancel with no deadline of its own. The publish goes through the Redis bus client's own timeouts, and terminateLocal bounds each release with userTerminateRPCTimeout. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:80-91,109-111
WATCHOUT: Shutdown order is httpSrv.Shutdown, then sessionSrv.Close. Spec §10 states no drain order for leases held against listeners. A forward listener closed "alongside" REST makes off-holder requests to a draining coordinator fail closed (409 or DLQ hold, which adoption delivers). That is a slower recovery, not a loss, so it does not meet the bar. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:213-230

### [non-spec.9.fix-G1.1]
FACT: The coordination fencer is a REG-COORDLEASE consumer. `coordfence.New(..., w.erasureLeaseStore, w.replica, ...)` is built in cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go, and its relinquish calls `f.leases.Release(ctx, tenantID, sessionID, f.replicaID)`. Both lease stores release only on an exact holder match (leasestore Lua `GET == ARGV[1]`, pgstore `AND holder = $3`), so a fencer left on the bare identity relinquishes nothing and returns no error. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:76-79; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence.go:220
DECISION: CODE-1 item 3 is the only list of consumers. TEST-1 item 2 points at it and names the fencer case (assert release behaviour, because `coordfence.Fencer` keeps `replicaID` unexported) — BECAUSE the duplicated list is how the fencer dropped out of both — ALTERNATIVES: adding the fencer to both lists (two copies drift again); a rule-based consumer definition (rewrites item 3, loses the file list).
FACT: The remaining non-test `w.replica` uses in cmd/lenny-gateway (ops emitter and Source in auxstores.go and runserver.go, the propagator Origin in adminrouter.go, the gateway control server in controlserver.go, ServiceInstanceID and the cb audit reporter in httpsurface.go, billing in stores.go, and the callback GatewayID in sessiondeps.go) are not lease or mirror consumers. httpsurface.go:585 `ReplicaID: w.replica` is the barrier mirror lister already in item 3. EVIDENCE: grep -n 'w\.replica\b' cmd/lenny-gateway/*.go
DECISION: CH-HOLDERFORWARD's listener file, flags, env vars, and port use the word-split stem: holder_forward_listener.go, --holder-forward-{advertise,listen}-addr, LENNY_HOLDER_FORWARD_{ADVERTISE,LISTEN}_ADDR, and container port `holder-forward` (14 chars, within the IANA_SVC_NAME limit of 15) — BECAUSE the §28.1 naming table flag precedent splits words (CH-RUNTIMEOPS gives `runtime-ops-socket`) and the proposal already names `holder_forward.go` — ALTERNATIVES: the unsplit `holderforward` stem (two stems for one channel in the same proposal).
WATCHOUT: `gateway.interReplicaPort` and the `#2854-inter-replica` anchor are still the boundary-named stem. The Helm key is staged in spec-changes.md (SPEC-2 (b), SPEC-7) and summary D4, so a non-spec round did not rename it, and N7 names flags, env vars, and adapter-manifest keys without clearly covering Helm values keys. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md N7; non-spec-changes.md CHART-1

- DECISION: an off-holder call on a WebSocket connection whose upgrade credential has expired gets the coordinator's 401 `TOKEN_EXPIRED`, recorded as an accepted failure mode (non-spec-changes.md Edge cases bullet, pinned in TEST-1 item 5) — BECAUSE the forward listener runs the same auth middleware, which rejects an expired JWT, and the client outcome (reconnect with a fresh bearer) needs no new mechanism — ALTERNATIVES: see the WATCHOUTs below.
- WATCHOUT: waiving `exp` on the forward listener to honor the §27.3.1 upgrade-time acceptance reopens revoked bearers, because §27.3.1 **5. Bearer revocation.** holds deny entries only for the bearer TTL (at most 15 min) and relies on `exp` afterwards; it would also need a SPEC-5 rule 3 exception — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:155
- WATCHOUT: closing the WebSocket with 4401 on an expired forward contradicts §27.3.1 **4. Refresh and rotation.** ("Existing WebSocket connections using the old bearer are **not** interrupted"), needs a staged §27.3.1 edit and a sessionserver-to-WebSocket path — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:149
- FACT: the auth middleware writes 401 `TOKEN_EXPIRED` only when `jwt.VerifyError.Reason == "expired"`; every other verify failure is `TOKEN_INVALID`, so `TOKEN_EXPIRED` is the assertion that discriminates against an expiry waiver — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:473-478
- OPEN [spec-changes.md Edge cases or §27.3.1]: the §27.3.1 Silent refresh bullet does not say that a call forwarded under staged §29.3 **Forwarding.** rule 3 needs an unexpired bearer. Spec-pass item.
- OPEN [non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 6]: what `ForwardToolCall` returns to the MCP client when the coordinator's answer is a non-JSON-RPC 401 auth refusal is unstated; this round did not specify it.
- USEFUL [non-spec.9.fix-design-G2]: the design's §27.3.1 and auth.go evidence was accurate and needed only re-confirmation.
