### [non-spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: The coordination fencer is a fifth REG-COORDLEASE consumer that takes the bare replica identity. `coordfence.New(..., w.erasureLeaseStore, w.replica, ...)` is in cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go (`if w.erasureLeaseStore != nil` block). Its relinquish calls `f.leases.Release(ctx, tenantID, sessionID, f.replicaID)`, and both lease stores release only on an exact holder match (leasestore.go Lua `GET == ARGV[1]`, pgstore `AND holder = $3`). If it is missed, relinquish silently does nothing. The other `w.replica` uses are not lease or mirror consumers: auxstores ops emitter, adminrouter propagator Origin, gwcontrol_server ServiceInstanceID, httpsurface ServiceInstanceID/cbAudit, sessiondeps callback GatewayID, and billing. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:76-82, pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence.go:218-220
WATCHOUT: `coordfence.Fencer.replicaID` is unexported and has no accessor, so a wiring test in cmd/lenny-gateway cannot read it. The fencer case of TEST-1 item 2 has to show the behaviour instead: after a relinquish, a lease held under the composite value is gone. EVIDENCE: coordfence.go:93-94,112-120
DECISION: Rename the CH-HOLDERFORWARD carriers to the word-split stem: `holder_forward_listener.go`, `--holder-forward-{listen,advertise}-addr`, `LENNY_HOLDER_FORWARD_{LISTEN,ADVERTISE}_ADDR`, and container port `holder-forward`. BECAUSE the only flag precedent in the §28.1 naming table splits the words (`CH-RUNTIMEOPS` gives `runtime-ops-socket`), and the proposal already spells the Go file stem `holder_forward.go` (CODE-1 item 8). ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's unsplit `holderforward_listener.go` / `--holderforward-*` was rejected because it would put two stems for one channel in the same proposal, which breaks N4's "one identifier everywhere".
DECISION: TEST-1 item 2 points at the CODE-1 item 3 list and no longer enumerates the consumers. BECAUSE a second enumeration is what let the fencer go missing from both lists. ALTERNATIVES: adding "the coordination fencer" to both lists keeps two copies of the list that can drift apart.
OPEN: `gateway.interReplicaPort` (Helm values key, staged in spec-changes.md SPEC-2 (b), SPEC-7, and summary D4) still spells the boundary rather than the channel. N7 covers flags, env vars, and §4.7 adapter-manifest keys, and does not clearly cover Helm values keys. Spec text in a non-spec round cannot rename it. A later spec round or a human should decide whether N7 reaches Helm values keys.
OPEN: TEST-1 item 2's second sentence ("The ops `Source`, billing, and `service.instance.id` keep `resolveReplicaID`") leaves out the §25.3 nonce checkpoint that CODE-1 item 3 keeps. It is a pre-existing partial restatement, and a later round should cut it to a pointer.

### [non-spec.9.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: an expired WebSocket upgrade credential on a forward is an accepted failure mode, stated once as a bullet in non-spec-changes.md `## Edge cases and accepted failure modes`, and pinned by one TEST-1 item 5 sentence. No change to CODE-1 item 7, SPEC-5 rule 3, or D5 — BECAUSE the forward relays the recorded credential unchanged and the coordinator's re-authentication (rule 3) fails closed with 401 TOKEN_EXPIRED, which forwardToHolder relays as a delivered response (no 409, no DLQ hold). That is already what CODE-1 item 7 builds, so no implementor builds anything different. What was missing is the record that the outcome is accepted, plus a test — ALTERNATIVES: (a) close the WebSocket with 4401: contradicts §27.3.1 step 4 ("not interrupted"), overloads the `bearer_revoked` close code, and needs a spec-changes.md edit, which this loop may not make. (b) Have the coordinator honour the upgrade-time acceptance, accepting an expired bearer when the peer marks it WebSocket-upgraded: this is a peer-asserted credential extension that any gateway-certificate holder could replay, and it breaks D5 and NET-072. (c) Have the serving replica mint a fresh token for the principal: a peer-asserted principal, which D5 forbids, and a new mechanism.
FACT: the WebSocket transport authenticates once at upgrade. Each frame is dispatched on a context derived from the upgrade request (watchCtx), and the only per-connection recheck is the §27.5 revocation poll, which applies to playground-origin bearers and closes with 4401. No frame-time expiry check exists. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcp/websocket.go:230-235 (dispatchFrameBytes(watchCtx, …)), :253-288 (startRevocationWatch); pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:473-476 (expired maps to 401 TOKEN_EXPIRED)
FACT: the playground client handles only close code 4401 (re-auth). A TOKEN_EXPIRED tool error surfaces as an ordinary error, so do not write "the client reconnects" in staged text. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/ui/app.js:791
WATCHOUT: ForwardToolCall (CODE-1 item 8 **MCP variant.**) says it "returns the JSON-RPC response". The coordinator's forward-listener auth middleware answers 401 TOKEN_EXPIRED, TOKEN_INVALID, or 503 as a §15.1 error envelope before /mcp runs, so for that path no JSON-RPC response exists. How the site maps a non-JSON-RPC answer to a tool error is unstated. This is a separate finding; it affects every auth refusal on the MCP forward, not only expiry. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **MCP variant.**; pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:473-478
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: §27.3.1 step 4 ("they continue running under the token they were upgraded with until the connection drops or the token's underlying session expiry is hit", spec/27_web-playground.md:149) is narrowed once SPEC-5 rule 3 lands. True instead: a call on such a connection that the serving replica forwards is re-authenticated by the coordinator and fails with 401 TOKEN_EXPIRED after the upgrade bearer's own TTL (default 900 s), while calls served locally continue. Remedy: a bullet in spec-changes.md `## Edge cases and accepted failure modes` citing §29.3 **Forwarding.** rule 3 and §27.3.1 step 4. If the pass stages it, cut the non-spec Edge cases bullet added by this design to a pointer at it, so the case has one home.
### [non-spec.9.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: An expired WebSocket upgrade credential on an off-holder forward is an accepted failure mode: the coordinator keeps the full §10.2 check and answers 401 `TOKEN_EXPIRED`; one Edge-cases bullet in non-spec-changes.md plus one TEST-1 item 5 clause pin it. CODE-1 item 7, SPEC-5 rule 3, and D5 are unchanged — BECAUSE waiving expiry at the forward listener (the "honor the upgrade acceptance" option) would bring revoked bearers back: §27.3.1 **5. Bearer revocation.** keeps a deny entry only for the bearer TTL (at most 15 min) and relies on `exp` to reject the token after that. A forward listener that skips `exp` would accept a revoked bearer once its entry has aged out — ALTERNATIVES: 4401 close on a forward refusal (contradicts §27.3.1 "Existing WebSocket connections ... are **not** interrupted", needs a staged §27.3.1 edit and a new path from sessionserver back to the mcp WS connection); relaying the upgrade acceptance as a peer assertion (conflicts with SPEC-5 rule 3 / NET-072 "accepts no other principal the forwarding replica asserts" and with 0097's posture that a gateway cannot mint or extend tokens).
FACT: A WS MCP connection authenticates once, at upgrade. Frames run on the upgrade context, and nothing re-checks `exp`. Only the playground revocation watch can close the connection mid-stream (4401). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcp/mcp.go:231-237 (wsAuth), pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcp/wscarrier.go:55-58; an expired JWT gets 401 TOKEN_EXPIRED at pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:473-475.
FACT: `TOKEN_EXPIRED` appears in no spec error catalog (spec/ hits only `UPLOAD_TOKEN_EXPIRED`). It is a code-only code. Do not cite it as a §15.1 catalog entry.
WATCHOUT: A later round will propose "skip exp on the mTLS forward listener, since the serving replica already checked it". It reopens revoked bearers after their deny entry expires. EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md §27.3.1 step 5 "Entries are held for the bearer TTL (at most 15 min)".
OPEN: §27.3.1 **4. Refresh and rotation.** "Silent refresh" says nothing about the fact that a call forwarded under staged §29.3 **Forwarding.** rule 3 needs an unexpired bearer. Its spec-level home is the spec-changes.md **Edge cases and accepted failure modes** list (cite rule 3 and §27.3.1) or a §27.3.1 clause, and the spec pass decides. This was not staged here because this loop does not edit spec-changes.md.
OPEN: CODE-1 item 8 **MCP variant** says `ForwardToolCall` "returns the JSON-RPC response". A coordinator auth refusal (401 for no credential or an expired one) is an HTTP error envelope rather than a JSON-RPC response, and no deliverable says what `ForwardToolCall` returns for it. This is a separate finding for a later round. It already applies to TEST-1 item 5's no-credential case.

### [non-spec.9.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: In a gateway started without `--agent-namespace` (every tests/testinfra/gateway subprocess, docker-compose), `w.podBinder` stays nil, so `coordinatesSession` returns true before any lease read, and no at-bind `acquireCoordinationLease` runs (it is called only on a bound pod). CODE-1 item 8 **Lease read** shares that guard, so `forwardToHolder` returns `ForwardLocal` on every request in the tier-4 subprocess lane. No forward, no hold, and no cross-replica coordination can happen there — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:151,424; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:88-91; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:483-494
WATCHOUT: The only lane with a pod binder and a lease store on two replicas is tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness (harness.go:334-340, PodBinder, CoordinationLeaseStore, and ReplicaID per replica). Kind has a binder but no forwarder (mtls.enabled false, devMode true). Any test that needs a real cross-replica forward or hold belongs in the lifecycleharness lane. The subprocess lane can only exercise a direct request to the forward listener (TEST-2 item 5) — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:42-43
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]: its WATCHOUT says "Forward coverage end to end is TEST-2's tier-4 two-subprocess lane with real certificates". That lane never forwards, because the subprocess gateways have no pod binder (see FACT above).
FACT: The MCP in-handler idempotency path (`mcp.Server.dispatchIdempotent`) covers only `lenny/create_session` and `lenny/delegate_task`. A forwarded `lenny/send_message` or `lenny/cancel_session` never meets it on the coordinator. The REST `idemmw` AllowedPaths include `/mcp` and `/v1/sessions/{id}/resume` — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:710-745
FACT: Go's http.Transport retries a request on a fresh connection only when the request is replayable (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS/TRACE, or Idempotency-Key present, with GetBody). CODE-1 item 8 copies no Idempotency-Key, so a forwarded POST or DELETE is never silently retried by the transport. "The helper never retries" holds.
USEFUL [spec.7.review-client-surface.1]: the compose advertise OPEN was checked again. The history already refutes it on materiality (one compose gateway is always the holder), so it was not re-filed.

### [non-spec.9.review-performance.1]
DECISION: No performance or failure-mode finding in round 9 — BECAUSE the round-8 edits (exported ForwardKind/ForwardOutcome names, bracket stripping in coordinationHolder, TEST-1/TEST-2 idempotency consolidation) add no store write, no new watch, and no new failure path; the Redis-reset window, idle-pool handshake cost, and listener server timeouts were already judged in earlier rounds — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the Redis-reset "no holder on the holder itself" window, rejected as settled (see USEFUL below).
USEFUL [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]: its Failover.Get trace (primary ErrNotFound is returned without consulting Postgres; Sweeper re-acquires within the 15 s default interval) saved re-deriving the Redis-reset behaviour.
FACT: CODE-3's tenant-wide drain publishes all transitioned IDs in one JSON pub/sub message (propagator.Request.SessionIDs). At about 38 bytes per ID, 100k sessions is about 3.8 MB, which stays under Redis's default pubsub client-output-buffer soft limit of 8 MB per 60 s. Each subscriber iterates only its own registry snapshot — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podterminate/propagator/propagator.go:47-52,113-130; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:114-134
FACT: docker-compose.yml defines a single `gateway` service with LENNY_DEV_MODE "true", so the compose advertise-address OPEN stays inert (no peer ever dials it) — EVIDENCE: docker-compose.yml:112,124

### [non-spec.9.review-reliability.1]
WATCHOUT: A WebSocket MCP connection outlives its bearer. §27.3.1 says existing WebSocket connections "continue running under the token they were upgraded with until the connection drops" after the bearer expires (default `playground.bearerTtlSeconds` 900). CODE-1 item 7 forwards the credential recorded at upgrade, and the coordinator re-authenticates it under rule 3. The auth middleware answers 401 TOKEN_EXPIRED on an expired JWT, so every off-holder call from a WebSocket older than its bearer fails while a local call keeps working. Filed this round. EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:149, pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:474-475, non-spec-changes.md:67, :242
FACT: The REST http.Server sets ReadHeaderTimeout 10s (cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:979). Shutdown order is httpSrv.Shutdown and then sessionSrv.Close, which run on stopCh after preStop. A forward listener drained "alongside" the REST listener therefore stays up through the preStop stages, while the replica still coordinates its sessions. That ordering was not filed. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:211-223
FACT: The forwarded POST/DELETE requests carry no Idempotency-Key, so net/http's Transport does not replay them on a reused connection, and "the helper never retries" holds. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:73
OPEN: The compose advertise address (service name, against the pod IP in SPEC-2 (b)) was refuted on materiality in the r9 history, so it was not re-filed. With a single gateway in compose, nothing dials that address.
USEFUL [non-spec Standing context "Crash takeover"]: This entry and review-log.md:743/1166 (Failover.Get bounded "no holder" window) settled the Redis-failover leg of the lease read without a re-derivation.

### [non-spec.9.review-security.1]
FACT: The public REST chain is, from inner to outer: idempotency, the circuit-breaker gate (cbmw), ratelimit, environmentmw, auth (with the PreAuth policyChain), metrics, deprecation, recover, correlation, and WebSocketBearerCarrier. CODE-1 item 6's forward-listener chain omits cbmw and environmentmw as well as the rule-4 pair. That is safe, because the forward point sits inside the handler, after the serving replica's cbmw has already run. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:721-974
FACT: sessionserver resolves the tenant from Principal.TenantID first, and the X-Lenny-Tenant-ID header only in dev mode. A calling-session request that skips auth therefore cannot inherit a header-borne tenant outside dev mode. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/request_identity.go:56-71
FACT: The existing allow-gateway-ingress rules are all port-restricted, so CHART-1's new 50054 self-peer rule is the only rule that admits traffic on that port. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:648-698
FACT: MCP-level idempotency (mcp.Server idem.Tools) covers only lenny/create_session and lenny/delegate_task. Forwarded lenny/cancel_session and lenny/send_message never reach dispatchIdempotent. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:715-718
USEFUL [review-log.md:751 gateway.spiffe WATCHOUT]: the per-replica SPIFFE CSI cert has no gateway DNS SAN. This defect predates 0101, so I did not re-derive it.
DECISION: no security findings in round 9. BECAUSE the trust anchors hold: the holder address comes from a gateway-written lease, the client verifies ServerName and the CA, the listener validates the SAN, the forwarded mark comes only from the listener, the sender-keyed limits are counted on the serving gateway, and PreToolResult runs on the serving replica's handleToolCall/DispatchTool over the returned result. The compose advertise-address OPEN stays refuted on materiality: only a single compose gateway runs. ALTERNATIVES: filing the missing X-Lenny-* header strip on the calling-session bypass. Rejected as hypothetical hardening: the peer is an mTLS-authenticated gateway, and the outbound request copies no such header.
